//! Reserved words: Quarry keywords cannot name a column or a binding. The
//! refusal names the word at its declaration, before a later use fails.

use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Site};

/// Words that begin a table line (`by`, `read <-`, `after insert`, …).
pub(crate) const TABLE_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
    "after", "by", "cap", "delete", "deliver", "ephemeral", "expire", "immutable", "insert", "online",
    "public", "read", "retain", "search", "sync", "unique", "update",
];

/// Words a statement or expression reads as syntax, so a parameter, local or
/// loop variable of that name cannot be used (or silently means the keyword).
// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — commit/new/now/viewer are contextual builtins, so declarations cannot shadow them.
pub(crate) const BINDING_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
    "and", "as", "break", "commit", "continue", "created", "delete", "device", "elif", "else", "emit",
    "exists", "false", "for", "if", "insert", "merge", "new", "not", "now", "null", "oblivious", "or",
    "require", "return", "true", "update", "upsert", "viewer", "walk", "while",
];

fn rename(word: &str) -> String {
    match word {
        "read" => "isRead".into(),
        "by" => "author".into(),
        "after" => "afterAt".into(),
        "update" => "change".into(),
        "delete" => "removal".into(),
        "insert" => "addition".into(),
        "public" => "isPublic".into(),
        "unique" => "isUnique".into(),
        "sync" => "synced".into(),
        "cap" => "capacity".into(),
        "expire" => "expiresAt".into(),
        "not" => "startsAt".into(),
        "online" => "isOnline".into(),
        "search" => "searchText".into(),
        "retain" => "retained".into(),
        "immutable" => "locked".into(),
        "ephemeral" => "transient".into(),
        "deliver" => "delivery".into(),
        "walk" => "outing".into(),
        "viewer" => "who".into(),
        "now" => "at".into(),
        "commit" => "committedAt".into(),
        "new" => "newId".into(),
        other => format!("{other}Value"),
    }
}

pub(crate) fn column(site: &Site, word: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let name = rename(word);
    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RESERVED",
        format!("`{word}` is a reserved word: it begins a table line (`{}`), so it cannot name a column", usage(word)),
        format!("rename the column, for example `{name}:`, and use `.{name}` in rules and queries (reserved words: snapback4 why E_QUARRY_RESERVED)"))
}

pub(crate) fn binding(site: &Site, word: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let name = rename(word);
    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RESERVED",
        format!("`{word}` is a reserved word, so it cannot name a parameter, local or loop variable"),
        format!("rename it, for example `{name}`, at this declaration and at each use (reserved words: snapback4 why E_QUARRY_RESERVED)"))
}

pub(crate) fn repeated_identity(
    site: &Site,
    first: (bool, bool),
    first_site: &Site,
    repeated: (bool, bool),
) -> Diagnostic {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — identity is project-wide and repeated declarations must name both method sets.
    let methods = |(password, guests)| match (password, guests) {
        (true, true) => "password and guests",
        (true, false) => "password",
        (false, true) => "guests",
        (false, false) => "no methods",
    };
    Diagnostic::new(
        site,
        "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX",
        format!("`use identity` is project-wide: {} already declared at {first_site}; this repeated declaration names {}", methods(first), methods(repeated)),
        "keep one declaration: `use identity` (password and guests), `use identity password`, or `use identity guests`",
    )
    .guide("production")
}

fn usage(word: &str) -> &'static str {
    match word {
        "read" | "insert" | "update" | "delete" => "read <- rule, insert <- rule, update <- rule, delete <- rule",
        "by" | "unique" => "by name: columns, unique columns",
        "after" => "after insert <- exists …",
        "public" => "public 'why'",
        "sync" | "online" => "sync … / online only",
        "cap" => "cap N by column",
        "expire" => "expire at .column",
        "not" => "not before .column",
        "immutable" => "immutable columns",
        "retain" => "retain until-revoked",
        "ephemeral" => "ephemeral 5s",
        "search" => "search column",
        _ => "a table line",
    }
}

//! Shared type-directed positional point selection for programs and rules.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — identity wins; otherwise ordered compatible declared keys only.
use super::catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo};
use crate::{ast::{IndexDecl, ReadExpr}, diag::Diagnostic, types::Ty};

pub(super) fn candidates<'a>(info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, types: &[(Ty, bool)], synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>) -> Vec<&'a IndexDecl> {
    let identity = read.index.is_none() && types.len() == 1 &&
        (types[0].0.required() == Ty::Id || types[0].0.required() == Ty::Ref(read.table.clone())
            || (!types[0].0.is_optional() && matches!(types[0].0.required(),Ty::Row(t)|Ty::WriteRow(t) if t==read.table)));
    info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
        .filter(|index| match &read.index {
            Some(name) => &index.name == name,
            None if identity => index.name == "byId",
            None => index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len(),
        })
        // Explicit indexes keep their existing mismatch diagnostics and range semantics.
        .filter(|index| read.index.is_some() || types.iter().zip(&index.columns).all(|((ty, literal), column)| {
            compatible(ty, *literal, &column_ty(info, index, column))
        })).collect()
}

fn column_ty(info: &TableInfo, index: &IndexDecl, column: &str) -> Ty {
    if index.computed.contains_key(column) { Ty::Int }
    else if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()) }
    else { info.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown) }
}

fn compatible(ty: &Ty, literal: bool, expected: &Ty) -> bool {
    ty.comparable(expected)
        || (!ty.is_optional() && matches!((ty.required(), expected.required()), (Ty::Row(a) | Ty::WriteRow(a), Ty::Ref(b)) if a == b))
        || (literal && expected.is_identity())
}

pub(super) fn refusal(
    info: &TableInfo,
    read: &ReadExpr,
    types: &[(Ty, bool)],
    candidates: &[&IndexDecl],
    catalog: &Catalog,
) -> Diagnostic {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — lead a wrong-table positional lookup with the foreign reference's actual table.
    if candidates.is_empty() && let Some((position, source)) = types.iter().enumerate().find_map(|(position, (ty, literal))| {
        match ty.required() {
            Ty::Ref(source) | Ty::Row(source) | Ty::WriteRow(source) if source != read.table
                && !info.indexes.iter()
                    .filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
                    .filter(|index| read.index.as_ref().is_some_and(|name| name == &index.name)
                        || (read.index.is_none() && index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len()))
                    .any(|index| index.columns.get(position)
                        .is_some_and(|column| compatible(ty, *literal, &column_ty(info, index, column)))) => Some((position, source)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }) {
        let value = read.keys.get(position).map(|key| crate::print::expression(&key.value)).unwrap_or_else(|| "this value".into());
        let mut expected = info.indexes.iter()
            .filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
            .filter(|index| read.index.as_ref().is_some_and(|name| name == &index.name)
                || (read.index.is_none() && index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len()))
            .filter_map(|index| index.columns.get(position).map(|column| column_ty(info, index, column).name()))
            .collect::<Vec<_>>();
        expected.sort();
        expected.dedup();
        let expected = if expected.is_empty() { "a value accepted by a declared key".into() } else { expected.join(" or ") };
        return Diagnostic::new(
            &read.site,
            "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN",
            format!("`{value}` is a {source} reference, but {} key position {} expects {expected}; a reference from another table cannot select it", read.table, position + 1),
            format!("replace `{value}` with {expected}, and name the intended key columns or choose its index with `by`; if this is a population read, use `{} where .<column> = <value> first 20 by .id`", read.table),
        )
        .guide("reads");
    }
    let alternatives = if candidates.is_empty() && read.index.is_some() {
        info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>()
    } else { candidates.to_vec() };
    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.7 — `budgets['general']` (lane 020): a text value names the declared
    // text key far more often than a row id, so that named form leads (and is --fix's choice).
    let mut alternatives = alternatives;
    alternatives.sort_by_key(|index| index.columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id"));
    let mut changes = Vec::new();
    for index in &alternatives {
        // Same-column opposite-order indexes need the explicit name as well.
        let duplicate = info.indexes.iter().any(|other| other.name != index.name && other.unique
            && other.columns.len() == index.columns.len() && other.columns.iter().all(|c| index.columns.contains(c)));
        if read.index.is_none() { changes.push(crate::repair::ReadChange::NamedKey { columns: index.columns.clone(), index: duplicate.then(|| index.name.clone()) }); }
        // Unknown explicit indexes and computed keys retain verified legacy alternatives.
        changes.push(crate::repair::ReadChange::Index(index.name.clone()));
    }
    let message = if alternatives.is_empty() {
        format!("{} has no declared unique key accepting these positional argument types in order; name the key columns, or use where for a population", read.table)
    } else {
        format!("{} positional argument types leave {} candidate keys; name the intended key columns or choose a compatible unique index explicitly", read.table, candidates.len())
    };
    let diagnostic = Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN", message, "").guide("reads").repair_read(&read.site, changes);
    // The user explicitly opts into one context-verified choice with --fix.
    if read.index.is_none() && candidates.len() > 1 { diagnostic.mechanical() } else { diagnostic }
}

//! Bind key operands once, retaining authored sequencing and contextual literals.
use super::*;

impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
    pub(super) fn bind_key_parts(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, end: usize,
        keys: &mut Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
        for (position, part) in read.keys.iter().enumerate().take(end).skip(keys.len()) {
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let expected = part.column.as_deref().or_else(|| read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)).and_then(|i| i.columns.get(position)).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column));
            let (e, t) = match expected {
                Some(expected) => self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup, &expected)?,
                None => self.expr(&part.value, &mut setup)?,
            };
            self.sequence_setup(keys.iter_mut().map(|(_, e, _)| e), setup, out);
            let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
            keys.push((part.column.clone(), e, t));
        }
        self.contextualize_bound_keys(info, read, keys)?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Apply the selected index's identity context to operands already bound
    /// for type-directed positional selection. This changes only their
    /// retained IR/type and never evaluates the authored expression again.
    pub(super) fn contextualize_bound_keys(&self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr,
        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {
        if let Some(index) = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)) {
            for (position,(column,value,ty)) in keys.iter_mut().enumerate() {
                if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().or_else(|| index.columns.get(position).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|c| key_identity(info,c)) {
                    if let Expr::Literal(Value::String(text)) = value {
                        if text.is_empty() {
                            return Err(type_error(read.keys[position].value.site(), "an empty text literal cannot be an id", "use a non-empty id literal, or null"));
                        }
                        *value = Expr::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone()));
                        *ty = expected.required();
                    } else {
                        (*value,*ty) = self.row_identity(value.clone(),ty.clone(),&expected);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    // Mixed-key normalization names an already-bound positional prefix. Only
    // a literal acquires new context; compound expressions keep their IR/setup.
    pub(super) fn name_bound_keys(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr,
        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {
        for ((column, expr, ty), part) in keys.iter_mut().zip(&read.keys) {
            *column = part.column.clone();
            if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column))
                && matches!(&part.value, A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(_), .. }) {
                (*expr, *ty) = self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup::Body::default(), &expected)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn key_identity(info: &TableInfo, column: &str) -> Option<Ty> {
    let ty = if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()) }
        else { Ty::from_scalar(info.columns.get(column)?) };
    ty.required().is_identity().then_some(ty)
}

   759	    fn key_ty(&self, info: &TableInfo, index: &ast::IndexDecl, column: &str) -> Ty {
   760	        if index.computed.contains_key(column) { return Ty::Int; }
   761	        if column == "id" {

//! A private population cannot answer a cross-row constraint for its writer.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#5.1 — aggregate consumers retain scan authorization and admission.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::{engine::{admission, rules}, schema::{Constraint, RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Schema}};

impl BoundProgram {
    /// The shortest repair for a hidden-row population when one direct read
    /// disjunct exposes the viewer-owned slice.
    pub(crate) fn direct_viewer_repair(&self, schema: &Schema, problem: &admission::Problem) -> Option<String> {
        if problem.code != "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT" || self.program.kind != ir::ProgramKind::Mutation { return None; }
        let site = problem.page_site.as_ref()?;
        let scan = ir::scans(&self.program).into_iter().find(|scan| &scan.site == site)?;
        let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
        // Expiry can refuse an already-uniform scan for a different reason;
        // adding its existing viewer predicate would not repair that refusal.
        if rules::scan_visibility(schema, scan).uniform || rules::grant_dominated(schema, &self.program, scan) { return None; }
        let column = table.rules.read.disjuncts().iter().find_map(|rule| match rule {
            RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Viewer)
            | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(column)) => Some(column.as_str()),
            _ => None,
        })?;
        Some(format!(
            "First add `.{column} = viewer` to this read's `where` predicate; that directly selects the read-rule audience and is the smallest qualifying repair. "
        ))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.025#6-diagnostics-and-exact-documentation-changes — retain the admission repair before the qualified receipt-gap fallback.
    pub(crate) fn expiry_gap_diagnostic(&self, schema: &Schema, problem: &admission::Problem) -> Option<Diagnostic> {
        use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType;
        if problem.code != "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT" || self.program.kind != ir::ProgramKind::Mutation { return None; }
        let site = problem.page_site.as_ref()?;
        let scan = ir::scans(&self.program).into_iter().find(|scan| &scan.site == site)?;
        let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
        if !table.is_expiring() || !rules::scan_visibility(schema, scan).uniform { return None; }
        let scan_index = table.index(&scan.index)?;
        let ordinal = scan_index.components.get(scan.prefix.len())?.column()?;
        if table.columns.get(ordinal) != Some(&ColumnType::Int) || !scan.orders_field(schema, ordinal) { return None; }
        let (parent, parent_table) = table.indexes.values().filter(|index| index.unique).find_map(|index| {
            if index.components.len() != 2 { return None; }
            let columns = [index.components[0].column()?, index.components[1].column()?];
            let parent = if columns[0] == ordinal { columns[1] } else if columns[1] == ordinal { columns[0] } else { return None; };
            let ColumnType::Ref { table: parent_table } = table.columns.get(parent)? else { return None; };
            // Consider every covering key, in either column order. An earlier
            // key on an unpinned parent cannot hide the applicable one.
            let scope = scan_index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix)
                .find_map(|(part, key)| (part.column() == Some(parent)).then(|| key.scalar()).flatten())?;
            rules::prefix_visibility(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read, &[(parent, scope)].into()).uniform
                .then_some((parent, parent_table))
        })?;
        // A sender/ordinal equality may make the refused page uniform while
        // other positions in the parent group remain hidden. Never call those holes.
        // Uniformity proves one verdict for the scope, not that it is readable;
        // proving a dominating caller check needs control-flow/rule implication.
        let name = &scan.table;
        // Quarry comments keep the whole rewrite pasteable without hiding the
        // smaller repair that admits the author's original scan.
        let rewrite = format!("-- {}\n-- Fallback if the mutation must keep its prediction or effect ordering:\nfn skipExpired_{name}(scope: {parent_table}, initialPrefix: int, low: int):\n  prefix = initialPrefix\n  for i in range(0, 1024):\n    if prefix + 1 >= low:\n      break\n    if exists {name}[{parent} = scope, {ordinal} = prefix + 1]:\n      break\n    prefix = prefix + 1\n  return prefix", problem.rewrite);
        Some(Diagnostic::new(&self.sources.get(site)?.0, problem.code,
            format!("{}. For receipt gaps, try the admission repair below first. If the shape must stay refused, use bounded unique ordinal probes; a new schema can declare `{ordinal}: sequence by {parent}` with `unique {parent}, {ordinal}`. The helper's prefix justifies advancing a receipt only after the caller's own authority check (the membership check or the card's readable-message anchor). Call it inside the receipt merge, retain disjoint runs, and let an already-recorded run reach the helper again to resume after the 1,024-probe limit. For an unread badge use a row-keyed count; for general processing use a query or job (guide sequences)", problem.message),
            rewrite).guide("sequences"))
    }

    /// This is diagnostic selection only. The ordinary admission pass still
    /// owns the capped-scope case; neither path changes a rule or a scan.
    pub(crate) fn constraint_diagnostic(&self, schema: &Schema, problem: &admission::Problem) -> Option<Diagnostic> {
        if problem.code != "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT" || self.program.kind != ir::ProgramKind::Mutation { return None; }
        let site = problem.page_site.as_ref()?;
        if !self.constraint_domains.contains(site) { return None; }
        let scan = ir::scans(&self.program).into_iter().find(|scan| &scan.site == site)?;
        if rules::scan_visibility(schema, scan).uniform || capped_scope_decides(schema, scan) { return None; }
        let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
        let source = &self.sources.get(site)?.0;
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — render the repairs against the author's own table and pins (app-farm patterns 6, 19).
        let pins: Vec<(String, String)> = table.index(&scan.index).map(|index| index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix)
            .filter_map(|(part, key)| {
                let column = part.column()?.clone();
                let value = match key.scalar()? { Expr::Variable(name) if !name.starts_with('$') => name.clone(), _ => column.clone() };
                Some((column, value))
            }).collect()).unwrap_or_default();
        let name = &scan.table;
        let group = pins.iter().map(|(column, _)| column.clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let key = pins.iter().map(|(_, value)| value.clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — offer the direct viewer predicate before heavier structural repairs.
        let direct_audience = self.direct_viewer_repair(schema, problem).unwrap_or_default();
        let example = format!("declare one row per reserved unit with `unique resource, unit else TAKEN`, then insert those rows inside `{}`", self.program.name);
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#16.2 — wave 6 R2: `require not exists children…` before a delete is a zero count, not a capacity.
        let by = pins.iter().map(|(column, _)| format!(".{column}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let absence = (!pins.is_empty()).then(|| absence_require(&self.program.body, site)).flatten().map(|code| format!(
            "an absence check over every {name} row, whoever may read it, is a zero count (it discloses only the count per {group}, never who): declare `view {name}Count: count {name} by {by}` with `read <- allow`, then in `{}` write `require {name}Count[{key}] = 0 else {code}` in place of the `not exists` (before a delete of the parent, this guard also silences N_DELETE_RESTRICTED; guide recipes, \"Booking\", `removeSlot`). Otherwise, ", self.program.name)).unwrap_or_default();
        Some(Diagnostic::new(source, "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT",
            format!("`{}` can read only the {} its viewer may read (`{}`). A check over them would miss hidden {} rows, so it is refused. A point read of a full unique key (`exists {name}[…]`) is not refused, but it too sees only readable rows",
                self.program.name, scan.table, table.rules.read.quarry(), scan.table),
            format!("{direct_audience}{absence}for one known {name} row, use a point read by id (`{name}[id]`); for a global total, read a maintained aggregate whose explicit read rule admits this caller (public only if its totals may be public). use a constraint every write obeys, whoever wrote the clashing row: {example}; a clash rolls back the whole mutation. A hidden clash stays E_RULE; make the unit table public only if availability may be public, so a readable clash can return TAKEN. Or use a readable audience: check a public availability table if those rows may be public. {count}A `require` role guard does not make the population readable. See `snapback4 guide writes` for the per-night example", count = if pins.is_empty() || !absence.is_empty() { String::new() } else {
                // @ref LLP 3000.036#14.6 — wave 4 #15: the count view and the author's own capacity check, as code.
                let check = capacity_require(&self.program.body, site).map_or_else(|| format!("require {name}Count[{key}] < limit else FULL"),
                    |(op, bound, code)| format!("require {name}Count[{key}] {op} {bound} else {code}"));
                format!("For a count or capacity, keep a count whose read rule covers the writers (it discloses only the count per {group}, never who): `view {name}Count: count {name} by {by}` with `read <- allow`, then in `{}` `{check}` before the insert; the insert itself keeps the count current (guide indexes, \"Private lists\"). ", self.program.name)
            }))
            .guide("writes"))
    }
}

/// Keep the existing scope-pinning rewrite only when a declared bounded
/// group supplies enough deciding columns. Preserve the current pins and
/// ask the engine's rule analysis, including self-probe absorption, whether
/// pinning that scope gives one verdict. A cap on unrelated columns cannot
/// turn a private conflict check into a check over every writer's rows.
fn capped_scope_decides(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> bool {
    let Some(table) = schema.table(&scan.table) else { return false };
    let Some(index) = table.index(&scan.index) else { return false };
    let pinned: BTreeMap<String, Expr> = index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix)
        .filter_map(|(part, key)| part.column().zip(key.scalar()).map(|(column, value)| (column.clone(), value.clone())))
        .collect();
    table.constraints.iter().any(|constraint| {
        let Constraint::Cap { by: group, max } = constraint else { return false };
        if u64::from(*max) > snapback4_core::Ceilings::default().examined { return false; }
        let mut candidate = pinned.clone();
        for column in group { candidate.entry(column.clone()).or_insert_with(|| ir::var("$constraint_scope")); }
        rules::prefix_visibility(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read, &candidate.iter().collect()).uniform
    })
}

/// The `require count(page) OP bound else CODE` that consumes the refused
/// page, with `bound` printed when it is a local, a field of one, or a literal.
fn capacity_require(body: &[ir::Stmt], site: &str) -> Option<(&'static str, String, String)> {
    fn print(expr: &Expr) -> Option<String> {
        match expr {
            Expr::Variable(name) if !name.starts_with('$') => Some(name.clone()),
            // A local is renamed `$s2` for `s`; compiler temporaries are not locals.
            Expr::Variable(name) => {
                let local = name.strip_prefix('$')?.trim_end_matches(|c: char| c.is_ascii_digit());
                (!local.is_empty() && !["present", "probe", "bool", "row", "tmp", "pureCall"].contains(&local)).then(|| local.to_owned())
            }
            Expr::Field { base, name } => Some(format!("{}.{name}", print(base)?)),
            Expr::Literal(Value::Int(n)) => Some(n.to_string()),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    let counts = |expr: &Expr| matches!(expr, Expr::Builtin { builtin: ir::Builtin::Count | ir::Builtin::CountCapped, args } if matches!(args.as_slice(), [Expr::Scan(scan)] if scan.site == site));
    body.iter().find_map(|statement| match statement {
        ir::Stmt::Require { condition: Expr::Binary { op, left, right }, code, .. } if counts(left) => {
            let op = match op { ir::BinaryOp::Lt => "<", ir::BinaryOp::Lte => "<=", _ => return None };
            Some((op, print(right)?, code.clone()))
        }
        ir::Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => capacity_require(then_body, site).or_else(|| capacity_require(else_body, site)),
        _ => None,
    })
}

/// The refusal code of a `require not exists …` whose existence test reads
/// the refused page (`require not exists sightings[cat = cat] else HAS`).
fn absence_require(body: &[ir::Stmt], site: &str) -> Option<String> {
    fn reads(expr: &Expr, site: &str) -> bool {
        match expr {
            Expr::Scan(scan) => scan.site == site,
            Expr::Unary { value, .. } => reads(value, site),
            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => reads(left, site) || reads(right, site),
            Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => args.iter().any(|arg| reads(arg, site)),
            Expr::Block { value, body } => reads(value, site) || body.iter().any(|statement| matches!(statement, ir::Stmt::Let { value, .. } if reads(value, site))),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
    body.iter().find_map(|statement| match statement {
        ir::Stmt::Require { condition: Expr::Unary { op: ir::UnaryOp::Not, value }, code, .. } if reads(value, site) => Some(code.clone()),
        ir::Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => absence_require(then_body, site).or_else(|| absence_require(else_body, site)),
        _ => None,
    })
}

use snapback4_lang::compile;

#[test]
fn project_builtins_are_reserved_for_parameters_locals_and_loop_bindings() {
    for (source, name) in [
        ("query q(now: time):\n  return now\n", "now"),
        ("mutation m():\n  now = 1\n  return now\n", "now"),
        ("mutation m():\n  commit = now\n  return commit\n", "commit"),
        ("mutation m():\n  for new in range(0, 1):\n    return new\n", "new"),
        ("query q(viewer: principal):\n  return viewer\n", "viewer"),
        ("fn helper(viewer: principal):\n  return viewer\n", "viewer"),
    ] {
        let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
        let diagnostic = bound
            .diagnostics
            .iter()
            .find(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_RESERVED")
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{name}: {:?}", bound.diagnostics));
        assert!(diagnostic.message.contains(&format!("`{name}`")), "{diagnostic}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn repeated_identity_declarations_name_both_method_sets() {
    let same_file = compile(&[(
        "schema.q".into(),
        "use identity password\nuse identity guests\n".into(),
    )]);
    assert_eq!(same_file.diagnostics.len(), 1, "{:?}", same_file.diagnostics);
    let diagnostic = &same_file.diagnostics[0];
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.code, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.site.line, 2);
    assert!(
        diagnostic.message.contains("password") && diagnostic.message.contains("guests"),
        "{diagnostic}"
    );

    let across_files = compile(&[
        ("schema.q".into(), "use identity guests\n".into()),
        ("accounts.q".into(), "use identity\n".into()),
    ]);
    assert_eq!(across_files.diagnostics.len(), 1, "{:?}", across_files.diagnostics);
    let diagnostic = &across_files.diagnostics[0];
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.site.file, "accounts.q");
    assert!(
        diagnostic.message.contains("guests")
            && diagnostic.message.contains("password and guests"),
        "{diagnostic}"
    );
}

fn diagnostic(source: &str, code: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Diagnostic {
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    bound
        .diagnostics
        .iter()
        .find(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == code)
        .cloned()
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing {code}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics))
}

fn replace_and_compile(source: &str, authored: &str, replacement: &str) {
    let repaired = source.replacen(authored, replacement, 1);
    assert_ne!(repaired, source, "fixture does not contain {authored:?}");
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired)]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn sql_habits_name_quarry_order_and_emit_compiling_rewrites() {
    let count = "table logs:\n  kind: text <=20\n  public 'logs'\nquery total():\n  return count logs where .kind = 'error'\n";
    let d = diagnostic(count, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
    assert!(d.message.contains("aggregates take parentheses"), "{d}");
    replace_and_compile(count, "count logs where .kind = 'error'", &d.rewrite);

    let descending = "table logs:\n  at: time\n  public 'logs'\nquery recent():\n  return logs last 20 by .at desc\n";
    let d = diagnostic(descending, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
    assert!(d.message.contains("last 20 by .at") && d.message.contains("descending"), "{d}");
    replace_and_compile(descending, "logs last 20 by .at desc", &d.rewrite);

    let shaped = compile(&[
        ("schema.q".into(), "table logs:\n  at: time\n  public 'logs'\nquery recent():\n  return logs last 20 by .at desc\n".into()),
        ("shapes.q".into(), "shape desc = { id }\n".into()),
    ]);
    assert!(shaped.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", shaped.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn cross_table_identity_names_the_wrong_reference_before_scan_advice() {
    let source = "table secrets:\n  public 'secrets'\ntable rolls:\n  public 'rolls'\nquery wrong(secret: secrets):\n  r = secrets[secret] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  return exists rolls[r.id]\n";
    let d = diagnostic(source, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN");
    assert!(d.message.contains("`r.id` is a secrets reference"), "{d}");
    assert!(d.message.contains("rolls key position 1 expects a rolls reference"), "{d}");

    let ambiguous = "table secrets:\n  public 'secrets'\ntable rolls:\n  primary: secrets\n  backup: secrets\n  unique primary\n  unique backup\n  public 'rolls'\nquery wrong(secret: secrets):\n  return rolls[secret]\n";
    let d = diagnostic(ambiguous, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN");
    assert!(d.message.contains("candidate keys"), "{d}");
    assert!(!d.message.contains("keyed by its own id"), "{d}");

    let later_mismatch = "table secrets:\n  public 'secrets'\ntable rolls:\n  secret: secrets\n  number: int\n  unique secret, number\n  public 'rolls'\nquery wrong(secret: secrets):\n  return rolls[secret, 'bad']\n";
    let d = diagnostic(later_mismatch, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN");
    assert!(d.message.contains("no declared unique key accepting these positional argument types"), "{d}");
    assert!(!d.message.contains("is a secrets reference") && !d.message.contains("key position"), "{d}");

    let wrong_composite_source = "table users:\n  public 'users'\ntable secrets:\n  public 'secrets'\ntable rolls:\n  secret: secrets\n  number: int\n  unique secret, number\n  public 'rolls'\nquery wrong(user: users):\n  return rolls[user, 1]\n";
    let d = diagnostic(wrong_composite_source, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN");
    assert!(d.message.contains("`user` is a users reference") && d.message.contains("key position 1 expects a secrets reference"), "{d}");
    assert!(d.rewrite.contains("replace `user` with a secrets reference") && !d.rewrite.contains("rolls reference"), "{d}");
}

#[test]
fn positional_type_selection_keeps_row_to_id_context_after_choosing_by_id() {
    for key in ["row", "(row if choose else row)"] {
        let source = format!(
            "table items:\n  public 'items'\nquery present(id: items, choose: bool):\n  row = items[id] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  return exists items[{key}]\n"
        );
        let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{key}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    }
    for read in ["items['']", "items[''] by byId"] {
        let source = format!("table items:\n  public 'items'\nquery present():\n  return exists {read}\n");
        let d = diagnostic(&source, "E_QUARRY_TYPE");
        assert!(d.message.contains("empty text literal cannot be an id"), "{read}: {d}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn nested_single_candidate_reads_keep_the_bind_once_path() {
    let mut key = "id".to_owned();
    // Deep enough to expose recursive duplicate binding while staying below
    // the language's existing nested-assert diagnostic expansion ceiling.
    for _ in 0..6 {
        key = format!("(items[{key}] ! else NOT_FOUND).id");
    }
    let source = format!(
        "table items:\n  public 'items'\nquery nested(id: items):\n  return items[{key}]\n"
    );
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn invalid_search_keeps_the_table_symbol_for_later_references() {

    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.message.contains("search category requires a text or enum column on businesses")), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    assert!(!bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.message.contains("unknown table businesses")), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn multiline_require_rewrite_preserves_the_authored_refusal_code() {
    let source = "query choose(a: bool, b: bool):\n  require (a or\n    b)\n    else NOPE\n  return 1\n";
    let d = diagnostic(source, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
    assert!(d.rewrite.contains("else NOPE"), "{d}");
    assert!(!d.rewrite.contains("NOT_ALLOWED"), "{d}");
}

#[test]
fn reviewed_disclosure_silences_only_the_maintain_delta_note() {
    let source = "table events:\n  owner: principal\n  category: text <=20\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  update <- allow\nmaintain totals: count events by category\n  read <- allow\n";
    let warned = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(warned.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_MAINTAIN_DELTA"), "{:#?}", warned.notes);
    let reviewed = compile(&[(
        "schema.q".into(),
        source.replace("  read <- allow\n", "  read <- allow\n  disclosure reviewed 'product staff intentionally see category totals'\n"),
    )]);
    assert!(reviewed.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", reviewed.diagnostics);
    assert!(reviewed.notes.iter().all(|note| note.code != "N_MAINTAIN_DELTA"), "{:#?}", reviewed.notes);

    for line in [
        "  disclosure reviewed ''\n",
        "  disclosure reviewed 'first review'\n  disclosure reviewed 'second review'\n",
    ] {
        let rejected = compile(&[(
            "schema.q".into(),
            source.replace("  read <- allow\n", &format!("  read <- allow\n{line}")),
        )]);
        assert!(rejected.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX"), "{:#?}", rejected.diagnostics);
    }
}

#[test]
fn structural_fanout_leads_with_a_direct_viewer_predicate() {
    let source = "table bottles:\n  organizer: principal\n  read <- .organizer = viewer\n  update <- .organizer = viewer\ntable shares:\n  bottle: bottles\n  member: principal\n  organizer: principal\n  cap 120 by bottle\n  read <- .member = viewer or .organizer = viewer\n  update <- .organizer = viewer\nmutation cancel(bottle: bottles):\n  b = bottles[bottle] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require b.organizer = viewer else NOT_ORGANIZER\n  for share in shares where .bottle = bottle:\n    update shares[share.id] { member: share.member }\n";
    let d = diagnostic(source, "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT");
    assert!(d.rewrite.starts_with("First add `.member = viewer`"), "{d}");
}

#[test]
fn unreadable_probe_names_an_existing_require_read_path() {
    let source = "table notes:\n  author: principal\n  read <- .author = viewer\ntable gatecodes:\n  note: notes\n  unique note\n  read <- exists notes[.note, author = viewer]\nmutation setCode(note: notes):\n  row = notes[note] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require row.author = viewer else NOT_AUTHOR\n  if exists gatecodes[note]:\n    return 1\n  return 0\n";
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    let note = bound.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_UNREADABLE_PROBE")
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{:#?}", bound.notes));
    assert!(note.message.contains("require/read path already established a readable notes row"), "{note}");

    for guard in [
        "require not exists notes[note] else PRESENT",
        "require exists notes[note] or other else NOT_ALLOWED",
    ] {
        let source = format!("table notes:\n  author: principal\n  read <- .author = viewer\ntable gatecodes:\n  note: notes\n  unique note\n  read <- exists notes[.note, author = viewer]\nmutation setCode(note: notes, other: bool):\n  {guard}\n  if exists gatecodes[note]:\n    return 1\n  return 0\n");
        let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
        let note = bound.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_UNREADABLE_PROBE")
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{guard}: {:#?}", bound.notes));
        assert!(!note.message.contains("require/read path already established"), "{guard}: {note}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn delete_restrict_note_opens_the_matching_guide_section() {
    let source = "table parents:\n  read <- allow\n  delete <- allow\ntable children:\n  parent: parents\n  read <- allow\nmutation drop(parent: parents):\n  delete parents[parent]\n";
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    let note = bound.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_DELETE_RESTRICTED")
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{:#?}", bound.notes));
    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("guide writes --section \"On delete restrict\""), "{note}");
}
//! Read-only production and fixture authoring notes; never an execution gate.
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
use snapback4_lang::{bind::Bound, Diagnostic, Site};
use snapback4_core::{ir::{self, ProgramKind, ArgType}, schema::{RuleExpr, RuleTerm}, engine::validate, wire};
use crate::project::Project;

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#8 — positive bootstrap dependencies form a least fixed point.
pub(super) fn notes(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, fixture: Option<&String>) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    let mut reachable = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut system = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut writers = BTreeSet::new();
    for p in &bound.programs {
        for (table, effect) in ir::write_tables(&p.program) {
            if matches!(effect, "insert" | "upsert") {
                writers.insert(table.clone());
                if p.program.kind == ProgramKind::Job { system.insert(table); }
            }
        }
        // Native jobs are opaque to the authoring analysis; no absence claim.
        if p.program.native { return fixture_notes(project, bound, fixture); }
    }
    loop {
        let previous = reachable.len();
        for (name, table) in bound.schema.visible_tables() {
            if bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some() { reachable.insert(name.to_owned()); continue; }
            if !writers.contains(name) { continue; }
            let refs_ready = table.through.as_ref().is_none_or(|through| reachable.contains(&through.parent));
            if refs_ready && (system.contains(name) || possible(&table.rules.insert, &reachable)) { reachable.insert(name.to_owned()); }
        }
        if reachable.len() == previous { break; }
    }
    let sources = project.sources().unwrap_or_default();
    let mut notes = Vec::new();
    let blocked: BTreeSet<String> = bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name, table)|
        table.ephemeral.is_none() && bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_none() && !reachable.contains(name.as_str()))
        .map(|(name, _)| name.clone()).collect();
    let site = |name: &str| sources.iter().find_map(|(file, source)| source.lines().enumerate()
        .find(|(_, line)| line.trim_start().starts_with(&format!("table {name}:")))
        .map(|(line, _)| Site::new(file, line as u32 + 1, 1)))
        .unwrap_or_else(|| Site::new(&format!("table {name}"), 1, 1));
    if !bound.personas.is_empty() && !bound.schema.auth.password && !bound.schema.auth.guests {
        {
            let at = blocked.first().map(|name| site(name)).unwrap_or_else(|| Site::new("schema", 1, 1));
            notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&at, "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER",
                format!("persona-only: safe to ignore without `use identity`; no production identity{}", if blocked.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; review bootstrap for {}", blocked.iter().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")) }),
                "safe to ignore without `use identity`; when moving to production, add use identity password, map roles and relationships to account principals, and provision the first admin; snapback4 guide deploy --section first admin").guide("deploy"));
        }
    } else {
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#3 — root components explain blocked descendants once.
        let graph: BTreeMap<String, BTreeSet<String>> = blocked.iter().map(|name| {
            let table = bound.schema.table(name).unwrap();
            let mut deps = BTreeSet::new();
            if writers.contains(name) {
                dependencies(&table.rules.insert, &mut deps);
                if let Some(through) = &table.through { deps.insert(through.parent.clone()); }
            }
            deps.retain(|dep| blocked.contains(dep));
            (name.clone(), deps)
        }).collect();
        let closure = |name: &str| {
            let mut seen = BTreeSet::from([name.to_owned()]);
            loop {
                let next: BTreeSet<_> = seen.iter().flat_map(|name| graph[name].iter().cloned()).collect();
                let count = seen.len(); seen.extend(next);
                if seen.len() == count { break; }
            }
            seen
        };
        let paths: BTreeMap<_, _> = blocked.iter().map(|name| (name.clone(), closure(name))).collect();
        for root in &blocked {
            let component: BTreeSet<_> = paths[root].iter().filter(|dep| paths[*dep].contains(root)).cloned().collect();
            if component.first() != Some(root) || paths[root].iter().any(|dep| !component.contains(dep)) { continue; }
            let dependents: Vec<_> = blocked.iter().filter(|name| !component.contains(*name) && paths[*name].contains(root)).cloned().collect();
            let cycle = if component.len() > 1 { format!(" (bootstrap cycle: {})", component.iter().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")) } else { String::new() };
            let dependent = if dependents.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; {} depend on it", dependents.join(", ")) };
            notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&site(root), "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER",
                format!("{root}: no production writer can bootstrap this root{cycle}{dependent}; fixtures and /seed are dev-only"),
                bootstrap_recipe(bound, root)).guide("deploy"));
        }
    }
    notes.extend(fixture_notes(project, bound, fixture));
    notes
}
fn bootstrap_recipe(bound: &Bound, table: &str) -> String {
    let writers: Vec<_> = bound.programs.iter().filter(|p| ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(target,effect)| target==table && matches!(*effect,"insert"|"upsert"))).map(|p|p.program.name.clone()).collect();
    let missing = if writers.is_empty() { format!("missing mutation path: no mutation inserts {table}") }
        else { format!("missing service-seat bootstrap path through {} (their {table} insert rule depends on rows not yet provisioned)",writers.join(", ")) };
    format!("{missing}; add a bootstrap{table} mutation that requires `viewer = 'service:ops'`, allow that branch in {table}'s insert rule, and make its required probes readable to the seat. Mint `snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment`, then call the mutation with the real account principal and its bearer token. For synced rosters use the independent seat-or-member branch; snapback4 guide deploy --section first admin")
}
fn possible(rule: &RuleExpr, rows: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Deny => false,
        RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => true,
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, .. } => rows.contains(table),
        RuleExpr::And(items) => items.iter().all(|item| possible(item, rows)),
        RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|item| possible(item, rows)),
        RuleExpr::Eq(left, right) => {
            if let (RuleTerm::Literal(a), RuleTerm::Literal(b)) = (left, right) { return a == b; }
            let principal = match (left, right) { (RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(v)) | (RuleTerm::Literal(v), RuleTerm::Viewer) => v.as_str().ok(), _ => None };
            if let Some(name) = principal.and_then(|p| p.strip_prefix("service:")) {
                return !name.is_empty() && name.len() <= 64 && name.bytes().all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == b'_' || c == b'-');
            }
            if principal.is_some_and(|p| p.starts_with("dev:")) { return false; }
            left.references().chain(right.references()).all(|(table, _)| rows.contains(table))
        }
        // Unknown predicates are potentially satisfiable: this lint is not a theorem of reachability.
        RuleExpr::Ne(_, _) | RuleExpr::Compare(_, _, _) | RuleExpr::Not(_) => true,
    }
}
fn dependencies(rule: &RuleExpr, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, .. } => { out.insert(table.clone()); }
        RuleExpr::And(items) | RuleExpr::Or(items) => for item in items { dependencies(item, out); },
        RuleExpr::Eq(left, right) => out.extend(left.references().chain(right.references()).map(|(table, _)| table.to_owned())),
        _ => {}
    }
}
fn fixture_notes(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, fixture: Option<&String>) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    let path = fixture.map(std::path::PathBuf::from).unwrap_or_else(|| project.root.join("fixtures")).join(super::test_cases::FILE);
    let Ok(text) = std::fs::read_to_string(&path) else { return vec![]; };
    let mut notes = Vec::new();
    for (index, line) in text.lines().enumerate() {
        let line = line.trim();
        if line.is_empty() || line.starts_with("//") { continue; }
        let case: Result<serde_json::Value, _> = serde_json::from_str(line);
        // Shared cases may include operations added after the schema being checked.
        if case.as_ref().ok().and_then(|case| case["op"].as_str())
            .is_some_and(|op| !bound.programs.iter().any(|p| p.program.name == op)) { continue; }
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — any authored refusal case owns its deliberately invalid arguments.
        let expects_refusal = case.as_ref().ok().is_some_and(|case| {
            super::test_cases::validate(case).is_ok() && case["expect"].as_str().is_some_and(|expect| {
                !matches!(expect, "ok" | "complete" | "capped" | "sent")
                    && case["op"].as_str().is_some_and(|op| bound.programs.iter().any(|p| p.program.name == op))
            })
        });
        let check = || -> Result<(), String> {
            let case = case.as_ref().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let op = case["op"].as_str().ok_or("case needs op")?;
            let program = &bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == op).ok_or_else(|| format!("unknown operation {op}"))?.program;
            let empty = serde_json::json!({});
            let args = case.get("args").unwrap_or(&empty).as_object().ok_or("args must be an object")?;
            for (name, kind) in &program.args {
                if !args.contains_key(name) && !kind.is_optional() && !matches!(kind, ArgType::Cursor) { return Err(format!("{op}: missing argument {name}")); }
            }
            for (name, value) in args {
                let kind = program.args.get(name).ok_or_else(|| format!("{op} takes no argument {name}"))?;
                // Saved answers are runtime values; never claim a type failure for one.
                if saved(value) { continue; }
                let value = wire::from_json(kind, value).map_err(|e| format!("{name}: {e}"))?;
                validate::validate_arg(&bound.schema, kind, &value, name).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            }
            Ok(())
        };
        if let Err(reason) = check() {
            // An authored refusal expectation may deliberately supply values
            // outside the operation's accepted input domain.
            if expects_refusal { continue; }
            notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&Site::new(&path.to_string_lossy(), index as u32 + 1, 1), "N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT", reason,
                "update this case's arguments to the operation's current signature; run snapback4 test").guide("testing"));
        }
    }
    notes
}
fn saved(value: &serde_json::Value) -> bool {
    match value { serde_json::Value::String(s) => s.starts_with('$') && s.len() > 1, serde_json::Value::Array(items) => items.iter().any(saved), serde_json::Value::Object(items) => items.values().any(saved), _ => false }
}
//! `test.cases.jsonl` beside the fixture tables: the application's own
//! operations, in order, as named viewers, each with the outcome it expects.
//! One line is `{"as": "alice", "op": "addCard", "args": {..}, "expect": "sent"}`;
//! `expect` is `sent` (a mutation's default), `complete`, `capped`, `ok` (any
//! answer), or a refusal code (`E_RULE`, `NOT_FOUND`, a program's own `require`
//! code). A query's default is its `check` verdict: `complete`, or either
//! answer when `check` notes the query is never complete (N_NEVER_COMPLETE).
//! Optional `data` must equal the answer; `count` is the answer's length; `save`
//! names the answer so a later string argument `"$name"` or `"$name.field"`
//! reuses it. `as` is a declared persona, as on dev: an undeclared one is
//! refused E_AUTH.
use crate::project::persona_principal;
use snapback4_core::engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace;
use snapback4_core::ir::ProgramKind;
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Refusal, WriteState, wire};
use snapback4_lang::Bound;
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::Path;

pub const FILE: &str = "test.cases.jsonl";

/// Dev's cast check, for `test` and a local `run`: with `use personas`
/// declared, a bare name (or `dev:<name>`) outside the cast is refused as dev
/// refuses its header (E_AUTH); a principal id such as `user:…` passes as itself.
pub fn undeclared_persona(name: &str, declared: &[String]) -> Option<String> {
    if declared.is_empty() { return None; }
    let bare = match name.split_once(':') { None => name, Some(("dev", bare)) => bare, Some(_) => return None };
    (!declared.iter().any(|p| p == bare)).then(|| format!(
        "persona {bare} is not declared; `use personas {}` is this project's cast, and dev refuses {bare} with E_AUTH (401). Add {bare} to it, or use a declared persona (an outsider is a declared persona with no rows)",
        declared.join(", ")))
}

/// The persona a case without `as` runs as: alice when she is in the cast (or
/// there is none), else the first declared persona.
pub fn default_persona(declared: &[String]) -> &str {
    if declared.is_empty() || declared.iter().any(|p| p == "alice") { "alice" } else { &declared[0] }
}

/// One case as `test`'s query sweep reads it: which query, as whom, with
/// which arguments (`None` when they name a saved answer, which only the case
/// run has), expecting what.
pub struct Explicit { pub at: String, pub op: String, pub principal: String, pub args: Option<Json>, pub expect: Option<String> }

/// The cases file read leniently, before the sweep: a line that does not
/// parse is left to [`run`], which refuses it with its line number.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#15.2 — wave 7: the sweep runs a persona's own case arguments and labels a refusal its case expects.
pub fn explicit(fixture: &str, declared: &[String]) -> Vec<Explicit> {
    let Ok(text) = std::fs::read_to_string(Path::new(fixture).join(FILE)) else { return Vec::new() };
    fn saved(value: &Json) -> bool {
        match value { Json::String(text) => text.starts_with('$') && text.len() > 1, Json::Array(items) => items.iter().any(saved), Json::Object(fields) => fields.len() == 1 && fields.contains_key("$literal") || fields.values().any(saved), _ => false }
    }
    text.lines().enumerate().filter_map(|(index, line)| {
        let line = line.trim();
        if line.is_empty() || line.starts_with("//") { return None; }
        let case: Json = serde_json::from_str(line).ok()?;
        let op = case.get("op")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
        let persona = case.get("as").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or_else(|| default_persona(declared));
        let principal = if persona.contains(':') { persona.to_owned() } else { persona_principal(persona) };
        let args = Some(case.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({}))).filter(|args| args.is_object() && !saved(args));
        Some(Explicit { at: format!("{FILE}:{}", index + 1), op, principal, args, expect: case.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str).map(str::to_owned) })
    }).collect()
}

/// Runs every case; prints one line each and returns (passed, failed).
/// `sources` are the compiled files, to quote a refusing clause's line.
pub fn run(engine: &mut Engine, bound: &Bound, fixture: &str, sources: &[(String, String)]) -> Result<(usize, usize), String> {
    let path = Path::new(fixture).join(FILE);
    let text = match std::fs::read_to_string(&path) {
        Ok(text) => text,
        Err(error) if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok((0, 0)),
        Err(error) => return Err(format!("cannot read {}: {error}", path.display())),
    };
    let mut saved = BTreeMap::<String, Json>::new();
    let (mut passed, mut failed) = (0, 0);
    let mut verdicts_shown = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    let lines: Vec<(usize, &str)> = text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_, line)| !line.trim().is_empty() && !line.trim_start().starts_with("//")).collect();
    // Every line is checked before any runs: a misspelled key must not pass
    // silently (wave 5: `"rows":0` passed against one row), and a typo on the
    // last line should not wait for every earlier case.
    let mut cases = Vec::with_capacity(lines.len());
    for (index, line) in &lines {
        let at = format!("{FILE}:{}", index + 1);
        let case: Json = serde_json::from_str(line).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}"))?;
        validate(&case).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
        cases.push((at, case));
    }
    println!("test: cases from {FILE}");
    for (at, case) in cases {
        let Some(op) = case.get("op").and_then(Json::as_str) else { return Err(format!("{at}: each case names \"op\" (a query or mutation)")) };
        let Some(bound_program) = bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == op) else {
            let starter = super::init::starter_fixture(FILE).is_some_and(|starter| starter == text);
            let hint = if starter { format!("\n  hint: {FILE} is the todo starter's unmodified cases for list/add/toggle/remove; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it (write your own cases, `snapback4 guide testing`)") } else { String::new() };
            return Err(format!("{at}: unknown operation {op}{hint}"));
        };
        let program = bound_program.program.clone();
        let persona = case.get("as").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or_else(|| default_persona(&bound.personas));
        let principal = if persona.contains(':') { persona.to_owned() } else { persona_principal(persona) };
        // A query's default expectation is check's verdict: a noted never-complete page passes capped.
        let never_complete = (program.kind == ProgramKind::Query).then(|| never_complete(bound, bound_program)).flatten();
        let explicit = case.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str);
        let expect = explicit.unwrap_or(match program.kind { ProgramKind::Query if never_complete.is_some() => "ok", ProgramKind::Query => "complete", _ => "sent" });
        let label = case.get("label").and_then(Json::as_str).map(|l| format!(" — {l}")).unwrap_or_default();
        let raw_args = substitute(case.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({})), &saved).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
        let input = (|| -> Result<_, Refusal> {
            if let Some(why)=crate::persona_principals::arguments(&bound.personas,&program.args,&raw_args) { return Err(why); }
            let mut args = BTreeMap::new();
            let fields = raw_args.as_object().ok_or_else(|| Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "args must be an object"))?;
            for (name, value) in fields {
                let kind = program.args.get(name).ok_or_else(|| Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{op} takes no argument {name}")))?;
                args.insert(name.clone(), wire::from_json(kind, value).map_err(|why| Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("argument {name}: {why}")))?);
            }
            Ok(args)
        })();
        let input_refusal = input.as_ref().err().cloned();
        let args = input.unwrap_or_default();
        let trace = RefusalTrace::default();
        let context_viewer = principal.clone();
        let context = Context::new(principal, args).with_refusal_trace(trace.clone());
        // (outcome word, answer) or a refusal
        let mut unavailable = false;
        let got: Result<(&str, Json), Refusal> = if let Some(refusal) = input_refusal { Err(refusal) }
        else if let Some(why) = undeclared_persona(persona, &bound.personas) {
            Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", why))
        } else { match program.kind {
            ProgramKind::Query => match engine.query(op, context) {
                Ok(outcome) => { unavailable = outcome.unavailable; Ok((if outcome.complete { "complete" } else { "capped" }, wire::to_json(&outcome.data))) },
                Err(refusal) => Err(refusal),
            },
            ProgramKind::Job => return Err(format!("{at}: {op} is a job; cases run queries and mutations")),
            _ => match engine.mutate(op, None, context) {
                Ok(outcome) => match outcome.state {
                    WriteState::Sent { result, .. } => Ok(("sent", wire::to_json(&result))),
                    WriteState::Failed { why, .. } => Err(why),
                },
                Err(error) => Err(error.refusal()),
            },
        } };
        let mut problems = Vec::new();
        let mut notes = Vec::new();
        let shown = match &got {
            Ok((word, answer)) => {
                let admitted = *word == expect || expect == "ok" || (expect == "complete" && *word == "sent");
                if !admitted { problems.push(if expect == "capped" && *word == "complete" {
                    "now complete — update the case: replace expect: \"capped\" with expect: \"complete\" (or omit expect); readable pinned-parent guards can now certify this page".to_owned()
                } else { format!("expected {expect}, got {word}") }); }
                if *word == "capped" && let Some(note) = &never_complete {
                    if admitted && explicit.is_none() && verdicts_shown.insert(op.to_owned()) { notes.push(format!("capped is this query's check verdict: {note}")); }
                    if !admitted { notes.push(format!("hint: {note}; a capped answer still holds every row you may read: add \"expect\":\"capped\" (or drop \"expect\"), or pin the page as the note's rewrite says (snapback4 check --verbose)")); }
                }
                if let Some(data) = case.get("data") && data != answer {
                    problems.push(format!("data differs: got {answer}"));
                    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.12 — an unavailable answer is not a null value: say why it has none.
                    if unavailable { notes.push("null here is no answer (a client shows `loading`), not a null value: a ceiling cut it, or an `exists` over rows this viewer only partly reads found no row it may read, which cannot prove absence (snapback4 check --verbose)".into()); }
                }
                if let Some(count) = case.get("count").and_then(Json::as_u64) {
                    let length = answer.as_array().map(|a| a.len()).or_else(|| answer.get("items").and_then(Json::as_array).map(|a| a.len()));
                    if length != Some(count as usize) { problems.push(format!("expected {count} rows, got {}", length.map(|n| n.to_string()).unwrap_or_else(|| "a non-list".into()))); }
                }
                if let Some(name) = case.get("save").and_then(Json::as_str) { saved.insert(name.to_owned(), answer.clone()); }
                (*word).to_owned()
            }
            Err(refusal) => {
                let code = &refusal.code;
                if code != expect && !(expect == "denied" || expect == "refused" || expect == "failed") {
                    problems.push(format!("expected {expect}, got {code}: {}", refusal.message));
                    notes.extend(refusal_evidence(&trace, refusal, bound, sources, explicit, &context_viewer));
                }
                code.clone()
            }
        };
        if problems.is_empty() {
            passed += 1;
            println!("      ok   {at} {op} ({persona}) → {shown}{label}");
            if let Err(refusal) = &got && refusal.code == "E_INPUT" { println!("           {}", refusal.message); }
        } else {
            failed += 1;
            println!("      FAIL {at} {op} ({persona}) → {shown}{label}: {}", problems.join("; "));
        }
        if program.kind == ProgramKind::Query && trace.hidden() > 0 { println!("           hidden: {} (encountered row images filtered by read rules)", trace.hidden()); }
        for note in notes { println!("           {note}"); }
    }
    Ok((passed, failed))
}

/// The keys a case line may carry.
pub const KEYS: [&str; 8] = ["as", "op", "args", "expect", "data", "count", "save", "label"];

// @ref LLP 3000.036#15.3 — an unknown key is refused, never ignored: ignoring it was a false green.
pub(super) fn validate(case: &Json) -> Result<(), String> {
    let Json::Object(fields) = case else { return Err("each case is a JSON object: {\"op\":..,\"as\":..,\"args\":{..},\"expect\":..}".into()) };
    let valid = KEYS.iter().map(|key| format!("`{key}`")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
    for key in fields.keys() {
        if KEYS.contains(&key.as_str()) { continue; }
        let meant = match key.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str() {
            "rows" | "length" | "len" | "size" | "n" | "total" | "counts" => Some("count"),
            "arguments" | "params" | "parameters" | "input" | "inputs" | "arg" => Some("args"),
            "persona" | "viewer" | "user" | "who" | "principal" => Some("as"),
            "operation" | "query" | "mutation" | "name" => Some("op"),
            "result" | "answer" | "value" | "rowsequal" | "equals" => Some("data"),
            "expected" | "expects" | "outcome" | "state" | "code" | "status" => Some("expect"),
            "description" | "desc" | "comment" | "note" | "title" => Some("label"),
            _ => None,
        };
        let hint = meant.map(|key| format!(" (did you mean `{key}`?)")).unwrap_or_default();
        return Err(format!("unknown key `{key}`{hint}; a case takes only {valid} (snapback4 guide testing)"));
    }
    for key in ["as", "op", "expect", "save", "label"] {
        if let Some(value) = fields.get(key) && !value.is_string() {
            let hint = if key == "expect" && value.is_object() { "; compare the answer with `data` (the whole answer) or `count` (its length) beside `expect`" } else { "" };
            return Err(format!("`{key}` is a string, got {value}{hint}"));
        }
    }
    if let Some(name) = fields.get("save").and_then(Json::as_str) && (name.is_empty() || name.contains('.') || name.starts_with('$')) {
        return Err(format!("save alias {name:?} must be nonempty with no dot or leading dollar; use a name such as r1, then reference $r1 or $r1.field"));
    }
    if let Some(value) = fields.get("count") && value.as_u64().is_none() { return Err(format!("`count` is a non-negative integer, got {value}")); }
    if let Some(value) = fields.get("args") && !value.is_object() && fields.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str) != Some("E_INPUT") { return Err("\"args\" is an object".into()); }
    Ok(())
}

/// The query's N_NEVER_COMPLETE note, as `N_NEVER_COMPLETE at schema.q:L`.
fn never_complete(bound: &Bound, program: &snapback4_lang::BoundProgram) -> Option<String> {
    bound.notes.iter().chain(&program.notes)
        .find(|note| matches!(note.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE") && note.site == program.site)
        .map(|note| format!("{}: {} ({} at {}:{})", program.program.name, note.message, note.code, note.site.file, note.site.line))
}

/// Development evidence under a failing refusal: which layer refused, at which
/// line (LLP 3000.036 §3 B; the refusal itself never names the clause).
fn refusal_evidence(trace: &RefusalTrace, refusal: &Refusal, bound: &Bound, sources: &[(String, String)], explicit: Option<&str>, viewer: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    let lookup = |file: &str| sources.iter().find(|(name, _)| name == file).map(|(_, text)| text.clone());
    let cause = trace.take();
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    match cause {
        Some(cause) if cause.terminal => {
            let record = super::run_refusal::record(&cause, &bound.refusal_sites);
            if let Some(text) = super::run_refusal::explain(&record, &lookup) { out.push(text.trim_start_matches("dev: ").to_owned()); }
            // Keep the operation source explanation alongside private table-rule evidence.
            if refusal.code == "NOT_FOUND" {
                if let Some(site) = refusal.site.as_deref() {
                    let resolved = operation_site(bound, site);
                    if let Some(line) = super::run_refusal::quote(resolved.as_deref().unwrap_or(site), &lookup) {
                        if !line.contains("else NOT_FOUND") {
                            let at = resolved.as_deref().unwrap_or(site).trim_end_matches(":1");
                            out.push(format!("NOT_FOUND is the write target's answer at `{line}` ({at}): the row is missing, or this viewer cannot read it and the table's write rule refused (a hidden row answers NOT_FOUND, never E_RULE; guide refusals)"));
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            if refusal.code == "E_RULE" && (cause.clause.starts_with("unique ") || cause.clause.starts_with("cap ")) {
                let held = if cause.clause.starts_with("unique ") { format!("a row with this `{}` key exists that this viewer cannot read", cause.clause) } else { format!("the `{}` group holds rows this viewer cannot read", cause.clause) };
                out.push(format!("hint: {held}; your own code would disclose it, so the engine refuses E_RULE: expect E_RULE here, or make those rows readable to the viewer (guide rules)"));
            }
        }
        // A program's own `! else CODE` or `require … else CODE`, at its
        // source line. When that site is a `t[x] !` lookup and t's read rule
        // hid a row in this operation, the row exists but reads as absent to
        // this viewer: a later `require` never ran.
        cause => if let Some(site) = refusal.site.as_deref().filter(|_| refusal.rule.as_deref() == Some(refusal.code.as_str())) {
            // An engine write-target refusal carries an operation-relative site
            // (`removeSale:2`); resolve it to the source line the program wrote.
            let resolved = operation_site(bound, site);
            let line = super::run_refusal::quote(resolved.as_deref().unwrap_or(site), &lookup);
            let shown = match (&line, &resolved) {
                (Some(line), Some(at)) => format!("`{line}` ({})", at.trim_end_matches(":1")),
                (Some(line), None) => format!("`{line}` ({site})"),
                (None, _) => site.to_owned(),
            };
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — wave 5: a NOT_FOUND the program never names is the write target's answer, not "the program's own refusal".

   725	    fn guard(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, condition: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {

   734	        });

   736	            let Some(table) = schema.table(&read.table) else { continue };
   737	            if readable(catalog, schema, &read, known) { continue; }

   754	        }

   756	    fn absence(condition: &Expr, inverted: bool) -> Vec<ReadExpr> {
   757	        match condition {
   758	            Expr::Exists { read, .. } => {
   759	                let mut out = if inverted { vec![read.clone()] } else { vec![] };
   760	                out.extend(absence(&Expr::Read(read.clone()), false)); out
   761	            },
   762	            Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "not" => absence(value, !inverted),
   763	            Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "=" || op == "!=" => {
   764	                let pair = match (left.as_ref(), right.as_ref()) {
   765	                    (value, Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(b), .. }) | (Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(b), .. }, value) => Some((value, *b)),
   766	                    _ => None,
   767	                };
   768	                pair.map(|(value, b)| absence(value, inverted ^ (op == "=" && !b || op == "!=" && b))).unwrap_or_else(|| absence(left, inverted).into_iter().chain(absence(right, inverted)).collect())
   769	            }
   770	            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => absence(left, inverted).into_iter().chain(absence(right, inverted)).collect(),
   771	            Expr::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => {
   772	                let mut all = Vec::new(); let mut copy = (**condition).clone();
   773	                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if let Expr::Exists { read, .. } = expr { all.push(read.clone()); } });
   774	                all.extend(absence(then, inverted)); all.extend(absence(otherwise, inverted)); all
   775	            }
   776	            Expr::Unary { value, .. } | Expr::Member { base: value, .. } | Expr::Assert { value, .. }
   777	                | Expr::ShapeUse { base: value, .. } | Expr::Walk { seed: value, .. } => absence(value, inverted),
   778	            Expr::Array { items, .. } | Expr::Call { args: items, .. } => items.iter().flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted)).collect(),
   779	            Expr::Object { fields, .. } => fields.iter().filter_map(|field| field.value.as_ref()).flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted)).collect(),
   780	            Expr::Shape { base, fields, .. } => absence(base, inverted).into_iter().chain(fields.iter().filter_map(|field| match field {
   781	                ShapeField::Field { value, .. } => Some(value), _ => None,
   782	            }).flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted))).collect(),
   783	            Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Created { read, .. } => {
   784	                let mut values: Vec<_> = read.keys.iter().map(|key| &key.value).chain(read.seeks.iter().map(|key| &key.value)).collect();
   785	                values.extend(read.filter.as_deref()); values.extend(read.matching.as_deref());
   786	                values.extend(read.range.as_ref().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_deref()));
   787	                values.into_iter().flat_map(|value| absence(value, false)).collect()
   788	            },
   789	            Expr::Write { keys, fields, condition, .. } => keys.iter().map(|key| &key.value)
   790	                .chain(fields.iter().filter_map(|field| field.value.as_ref())).chain(condition.as_deref())
   791	                .flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted)).collect(),
   792	            Expr::Merge(merge) => {
   793	                let mut values: Vec<Expr> = [&merge.matching, &merge.filter, &merge.cursor].into_iter().filter_map(|value| value.as_deref().cloned()).collect();
   794	                for lane in &merge.lanes {
   795	                    values.extend(lane.admission.as_deref().cloned()); values.extend(lane.distinct.clone());
   796	                    values.extend(lane.prefix.iter().cloned()); values.extend(lane.loop_.as_ref().map(|(_, value)| value.clone()));
   797	                    for read in lane.planned.iter().chain(lane.nulls.as_ref().map(|(_, read)| read)) { values.push(Expr::Read(*read.clone())); }
   798	                    if let Some(fields) = &lane.project { values.extend(fields.iter().filter_map(|field| if let ShapeField::Field { value, .. } = field { Some(value.clone()) } else { None })); }
   799	                }
   800	                values.iter().flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted)).collect()
   801	            },
   802	            Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Focal { .. } | Expr::Literal { .. } => vec![],
   803	        }
   804	    }
   805	    // Reverse dependency closure identifies query values consumed by guards,
   806	    // across aliases/objects and all possible rebinding paths. The observation
   807	    // is checked at its original statement, with that statement's authority.
   808	    fn has_guard(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt], visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
   809	        if statements.iter().any(|stmt| matches!(stmt, Stmt::Require { .. } | Stmt::If { .. })) { return true; }
   810	        let mut copy = analysis_body(statements); let mut found = false;
   811	        super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
   812	            if matches!(expr, Expr::If { .. }) { found = true; }
   813	            for (name, shape) in definitions(catalog, expr) {
