//! Bind key operands once, retaining authored sequencing and contextual literals.
use super::*;

impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
    pub(super) fn bind_key_parts(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, end: usize,
        keys: &mut Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
        for (position, part) in read.keys.iter().enumerate().take(end).skip(keys.len()) {
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let expected = part.column.as_deref().or_else(|| read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)).and_then(|i| i.columns.get(position)).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column));
            let (e, t) = match expected {
                Some(expected) => self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup, &expected)?,
                None => self.expr(&part.value, &mut setup)?,
            };
            self.sequence_setup(keys.iter_mut().map(|(_, e, _)| e), setup, out);
            let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
            keys.push((part.column.clone(), e, t));
        }
        self.contextualize_bound_keys(info, read, keys)?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Apply the selected index's identity context to operands already bound
    /// for type-directed positional selection. This changes only their
    /// retained IR/type and never evaluates the authored expression again.
    pub(super) fn contextualize_bound_keys(&self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr,
        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {
        if let Some(index) = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)) {
            for (position,(column,value,ty)) in keys.iter_mut().enumerate() {
                if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().or_else(|| index.columns.get(position).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|c| key_identity(info,c)) {
                    if let Expr::Literal(Value::String(text)) = value {
                        if text.is_empty() {
                            return Err(type_error(read.keys[position].value.site(), "an empty text literal cannot be an id", "use a non-empty id literal, or null"));
                        }
                        *value = Expr::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone()));
                        *ty = expected.required();
                    } else {
                        (*value,*ty) = self.row_identity(value.clone(),ty.clone(),&expected);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    // Mixed-key normalization names an already-bound positional prefix. Only
    // a literal acquires new context; compound expressions keep their IR/setup.
    pub(super) fn name_bound_keys(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr,
        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {
        for ((column, expr, ty), part) in keys.iter_mut().zip(&read.keys) {
            *column = part.column.clone();
            if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column))
                && matches!(&part.value, A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(_), .. }) {
                (*expr, *ty) = self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup::Body::default(), &expected)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn key_identity(info: &TableInfo, column: &str) -> Option<Ty> {
    let ty = if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()) }
        else { Ty::from_scalar(info.columns.get(column)?) };
    ty.required().is_identity().then_some(ty)
}

    /// An expression in an identity position: a text literal becomes an id.
    pub(super) fn expr_as_identity(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        if let A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(text), .. } = expr {
            if text.is_empty() {
                return Err(type_error(expr.site(), "an empty text literal cannot be an id", "use a non-empty id literal, or null"));
            }
            return Ok((Expr::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone())), expected.required()));
        }
        self.expr(expr, out)
    }

    pub(super) fn expr_as_reference(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        let (value, ty) = self.expr_as_identity(expr, out, expected)?;
        Ok(self.row_identity(value, ty, expected))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — contextual projection is exactly `.id`,
    // without a lookup; optional and cross-table rows still need an edit.
    pub(super) fn row_identity(&self, value: Expr, ty: Ty, expected: &Ty) -> (Expr, Ty) {
        if !ty.is_optional() && matches!(ty.required(), Ty::Row(_) | Ty::WriteRow(_)) {
            let table = match ty.required() { Ty::Row(t) | Ty::WriteRow(t) => t, _ => unreachable!() };
            if expected.required() == Ty::Ref(table.clone()) {
                return (ir::field(value, "id"), Ty::Ref(table));
            }
        }
        (value, ty)
    }

    pub fn is_identity(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Id | Ty::Principal | Ty::Ref(_))
    }

    pub fn from_scalar(scalar: &ScalarType) -> Ty {
        let base = match &scalar.kind {
            TypeKind::Cursor => Ty::Cursor,
            TypeKind::Text { max, .. } => Ty::Text(*max),
            TypeKind::Id => Ty::Id,
            TypeKind::Principal => Ty::Principal,
            TypeKind::Int | TypeKind::IntRange { .. } => Ty::Int,
            TypeKind::Decimal { scale } => Ty::Decimal(*scale),
            TypeKind::Money { currency, scale } => Ty::Money(currency.clone(), *scale),
            TypeKind::Time => Ty::Time,
            TypeKind::Bool => Ty::Bool,
            TypeKind::Json { .. } => Ty::Json,
            TypeKind::Bytes { .. } => Ty::Bytes,
            TypeKind::Image { .. } => Ty::Image,
            TypeKind::Video { .. } => Ty::Video,
            TypeKind::File { .. } => Ty::File,
            TypeKind::Enum { variants } => Ty::Enum(variants.clone()),
            TypeKind::Ref { table } => Ty::Ref(table.clone()),
            TypeKind::List { item, max, .. } => Ty::List(Box::new(Ty::from_scalar(item)), *max),
        };
        if scalar.optional { base.optional() } else { base }
    }

    pub fn from_column(kind: &ColumnType) -> Ty {
        match kind {
            ColumnType::Id => Ty::Id,
            ColumnType::Principal => Ty::Principal,
            ColumnType::Text { max } => Ty::Text(*max),
            ColumnType::Bytes { .. } => Ty::Bytes,
            ColumnType::Image { .. } => Ty::Image,
            ColumnType::Video { .. } => Ty::Video,
            ColumnType::File { .. } => Ty::File,
            ColumnType::Int | ColumnType::IntRange { .. } => Ty::Int,
            ColumnType::Enum { variants } => Ty::Enum(variants.clone()),
            ColumnType::Decimal { scale } => Ty::Decimal(*scale),
            ColumnType::Money { currency, scale } => Ty::Money(currency.clone(), *scale),
            ColumnType::Bool => Ty::Bool,
            ColumnType::Time => Ty::Time,
            ColumnType::Ref { table } => Ty::Ref(table.clone()),
            ColumnType::Json { .. } => Ty::Json,
            ColumnType::Optional(inner) => Ty::from_column(inner).optional(),
            ColumnType::List { of, max } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, max, .. } => Ty::List(Box::new(Ty::from_column(of)), *max),
        }
    }

    /// The name a diagnostic prints.
    pub fn name(&self) -> String {
        match self {
            Ty::Unknown => "an unknown value".into(),
            Ty::Null => "null".into(),
            Ty::Bool => "bool".into(),
            Ty::Int => "int".into(),
            Ty::Time => "time".into(),
            Ty::Decimal(scale) => format!("decimal {scale}"),
            Ty::Money(currency, scale) => format!("money {currency} {scale}"),
            Ty::Text(max) => format!("text <={max}"),
            Ty::Id => "id".into(),
            Ty::Principal => "principal".into(),
            Ty::Ref(table) => format!("a {table} reference"),
            Ty::Bytes => "bytes".into(),
            Ty::Json => "json".into(),
            Ty::Image => "image".into(),
            Ty::Video => "video".into(),
            Ty::File => "file".into(),
            Ty::Enum(variants) => format!("enum({})", variants.iter().map(|v| format!("'{v}'")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
            Ty::Cursor => "cursor".into(),
            Ty::Optional(inner) => format!("{} ?", inner.name()),
            Ty::List(item, max) => format!("[{}] <={max}", item.name()),
            Ty::Row(table) => format!("a {table} row"),
            Ty::WriteRow(table) => format!("a {table} write result"),
            Ty::Page(table, max) => format!("a page of at most {max} {table} rows"),
            Ty::Collection(item, max) => format!("at most {max} of {}", item.name()),
            Ty::Object(fields) | Ty::RowShape(fields) | Ty::RowObject(fields) => format!("{{ {} }}", fields.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
        }
    }

    /// Whether a value of `self` may be stored where `expected` is declared.
    pub fn assignable_to(&self, expected: &Ty) -> bool {
        if self == expected {
            return true;
        }
        match (self, expected) {
            (Ty::Null, Ty::Optional(_)) => true,
            (Ty::Optional(inner), Ty::Optional(target)) => inner.assignable_to(target),
            (value, Ty::Optional(target)) => value.assignable_to(target),
            (Ty::Text(a), Ty::Text(b)) => a <= b,
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20 — enum widening is static and total: every variant fits the text bound and character domain.
            (Ty::Enum(variants), Ty::Text(max)) => variants.iter().all(|v| v.chars().count() <= *max as usize && snapback4_core::engine::validate::disallowed_text_control(v).is_none()),
            // A text literal in an enum position is a variant; the binder
            // checks membership where it knows the literal.
            (Ty::Text(_), Ty::Enum(_)) => true,
            (Ty::Id, Ty::Ref(_)) | (Ty::Ref(_), Ty::Id) | (Ty::Id, Ty::Principal) | (Ty::Principal, Ty::Id) => true,
            (Ty::Ref(a), Ty::Ref(b)) => a == b,
            (Ty::Int, Ty::Time) | (Ty::Time, Ty::Int) => true,
            (Ty::Int, Ty::Decimal(_)) | (Ty::Int, Ty::Money(..)) => true,
            (a, b) if a.sequence().is_some() && b.sequence().is_some() => {
                let (a, am) = a.sequence().unwrap();
                let (b, bm) = b.sequence().unwrap();
                a.assignable_to(&b) && am <= bm
            },
            (Ty::Object(a) | Ty::RowShape(a) | Ty::RowObject(a), Ty::Object(b) | Ty::RowShape(b) | Ty::RowObject(b)) => a.len() == b.len() && a.iter().all(|(name, ty)| b.get(name).is_some_and(|target| ty.assignable_to(target))),
    }
    fn guard_bindings(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt]) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        fn add_names(expr: &Expr, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
            if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr { out.insert(name.clone()); }
            if let Expr::Object { fields, .. } | Expr::Write { fields, .. } = expr {
                out.extend(fields.iter().filter(|field| field.value.is_none() && !field.spread).map(|field| field.name.clone()));
            }
        }
        fn names(value: &Expr) -> BTreeSet<String> {
            let mut out = BTreeSet::new(); let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| add_names(expr, &mut out)); out
        }
        fn input_names(value: &Expr) -> BTreeSet<String> {
            let mut out = BTreeSet::new(); value_nodes(value, &mut |expr| add_names(expr, &mut out)); out
        }
        fn gather(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt], guards: &mut BTreeSet<String>, edges: &mut BTreeMap<String, BTreeSet<String>>) {
            let mut copy = analysis_body(statements);
            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
                if let Expr::If { condition, .. } = expr { guards.extend(names(condition)); }
                // A definition can consume its input as a guard even when the
                // call itself is not a conditional. Conservatively track all
                // argument/base dependencies of such definitions.
                for input in guarded_inputs(catalog, expr) { guards.extend(input_names(&input)); }
            });
            for statement in statements {
                match statement {
                    Stmt::Bind { names: bindings, value, .. } => for name in bindings { edges.entry(name.clone()).or_default().extend(names(value)); },
                    Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => guards.extend(names(condition)),
                    Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, .. } => { guards.extend(names(condition)); gather(catalog, then_body, guards, edges); gather(catalog, else_body, guards, edges); }
                    Stmt::For { name, domain, body, .. } => { edges.entry(name.clone()).or_default().extend(names(domain)); gather(catalog, body, guards, edges); }
                    Stmt::As { body, .. } => gather(catalog, body, guards, edges),
                    _ => {}
                }
            }
        }
        let mut guards = BTreeSet::new(); let mut edges = BTreeMap::new(); gather(catalog, statements, &mut guards, &mut edges);
        loop {
            let count = guards.len();
            let next: Vec<_> = guards.iter().filter_map(|name| edges.get(name)).flat_map(|values| values.iter().cloned()).collect();
            guards.extend(next); if count == guards.len() { return guards; }
        }
    }
    fn helper_body(catalog: &Catalog, name: &str, shape: bool) -> Option<Vec<Stmt>> {
        if shape {
            let decl = catalog.shapes.get(name)?;
            Some(decl.fields.iter().filter_map(|field| match field {
                ShapeField::Field { value, site, .. } => Some(Stmt::Expr { value: value.clone(), site: site.clone() }),
                ShapeField::NamedSpread { name, site } => Some(Stmt::Expr { value: Expr::ShapeUse { base: Box::new(Expr::Focal { site: site.clone() }), name: name.clone(), site: site.clone() }, site: site.clone() }),
                _ => None,
            }).collect())
        } else {
            let helper = catalog.functions.get(name)?;
            Some(match &helper.body {
                FunctionBody::Block(body) => body.clone(),
                FunctionBody::Expression(value) => vec![Stmt::Return { value: value.clone(), site: value.site().clone() }],
            })
        }
    }
    fn definitions(catalog: &Catalog, expr: &Expr) -> Vec<(String, bool)> {
        match expr {
            Expr::Call { name, .. } if catalog.functions.contains_key(name) => vec![(name.clone(), false)],
            Expr::ShapeUse { name, .. } => vec![(name.clone(), true)],
            Expr::Name { name, .. } if catalog.shapes.contains_key(name) => vec![(name.clone(), true)],
            Expr::Shape { fields, .. } => fields.iter().filter_map(|field| if let ShapeField::NamedSpread { name, .. } = field { Some((name.clone(), true)) } else { None }).collect(),
            _ => vec![],
        }
    }
    fn effects(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt], visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> (bool, bool) {
        let mut copy = analysis_body(statements);
        let mut writes = false;
        let mut binds = false;
        fn bindings(body: &[Stmt]) -> bool {
            body.iter().any(|stmt| match stmt {
                Stmt::Bind { .. } => true,
                Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => bindings(then_body) || bindings(else_body),
                Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => bindings(body),
                _ => false,
            })
        }
        binds |= bindings(statements);
        super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
            if matches!(expr, Expr::Write { .. }) { writes = true; }
            for (name, shape) in definitions(catalog, expr) {
                let key = format!("{shape}:{name}");
                if !visiting.insert(key.clone()) { writes = true; binds = true; continue; }
                if let Some(body) = helper_body(catalog, &name, shape) {
                    let (w, b) = effects(catalog, &body, visiting); writes |= w; binds |= b;
                } else { writes = true; binds = true; }
                visiting.remove(&key);
            }
        });
        (writes, binds)
    }
    fn effectful(catalog: &Catalog, value: &Expr) -> bool {
        effects(catalog, &[Stmt::Expr { value: value.clone(), site: value.site().clone() }], &mut BTreeSet::new()).0
    }
    fn ordered_call(catalog: &Catalog, value: &Expr) -> bool {
        matches!(value, Expr::Call { name, args, .. } if catalog.functions.contains_key(name) && args.iter().all(|arg| !effectful(catalog, arg)))
    }
    fn call_facts(catalog: &Catalog, name: &str, args: &[Expr], known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        let mut out: BTreeSet<_> = known.intersection(global).cloned().collect();
        let Some(helper) = catalog.functions.get(name) else { return out };
        let readonly = helper_body(catalog, name, false).is_some_and(|body| !effects(catalog, &body, &mut BTreeSet::new()).0);
        // Use fresh intermediate names so caller variables that happen to share
        // a formal's spelling cannot be mistaken for transferred evidence.
        for fact in known {
            let source = format!("query guardFact():\n  require {fact} else GUARD\n  return null\n");
            let (doc, errors) = crate::parser::parse_document("guard.q", &source);
            if !errors.is_empty() { continue; }
            let Some(Stmt::Require { condition, .. }) = doc.programs.first().and_then(|p| p.body.first()) else { continue };
            let mut condition = condition.clone();
            let mut used = BTreeSet::new();
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| { if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr { used.insert(name.clone()); } });
            used.extend(helper.args.iter().map(|param| param.name.clone()));
            for arg in args {
                let mut copy = analysis_expr(arg);
                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr { used.insert(name.clone()); } });
            }
            let mut substitutions = Vec::new();
            for (param, arg) in helper.args.iter().zip(args) {
                if is_viewer(arg) || (!readonly && !matches!(arg, Expr::Name { .. })) { continue; }
                let mut marker = "diagSnapshot".to_owned();
                while used.contains(&marker) { marker.push('_'); }
                used.insert(marker.clone());
                substitutions.push((fact_text(arg), marker, param.name.clone()));
            }
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| {
                let text = fact_text(expr);
                if let Some((_, marker, _)) = substitutions.iter().find(|(arg, _, _)| *arg == text) {
                    *expr = Expr::Name { name: marker.clone(), site: expr.site().clone() };
                }
            });
            let mut valid = true;
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| {
                if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr && name != "viewer" && !substitutions.iter().any(|(_, marker, _)| marker == name) { valid = false; }
                if matches!(expr, Expr::Focal { .. }) { valid = false; }
            });
            if valid {
                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| {
                    if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr && let Some((_, _, param)) = substitutions.iter().find(|(_, marker, _)| marker == name) { *name = param.clone(); }
                });
                out.insert(fact_text(&condition));
            }
        }
        for (param, arg) in helper.args.iter().zip(args) {
            if is_viewer(arg) { out.insert(format!("({} = viewer)", param.name)); }
        }
        out
    }
    fn rebound_names(statements: &[Stmt], out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        for statement in statements {
            match statement {
                Stmt::Bind { names, .. } => out.extend(names.iter().cloned()),
                Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => { rebound_names(then_body, out); rebound_names(else_body, out); }
                Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => rebound_names(body, out),
                _ => {}
            }
        }
    }
    fn lookup_fact(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, names: &[String], value: &Expr, known: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        let mut shadowed = false;
        let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if matches!(expr, Expr::Name { name, .. } if names.contains(name)) { shadowed = true; } });
        if shadowed { return; }
        let Expr::Assert { value, .. } = value else { return };
        let Expr::Read(read) = value.as_ref() else { return };
        if schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some() || !point_probe(catalog, read) { return; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        let mut indexes: Vec<_> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) } else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() }).collect();
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && read.keys.len() == 1 && is_viewer(&read.keys[0].value) {
            indexes.retain(|index| info.columns.get(&index.columns[0]).is_some_and(|kind| kind.kind == TypeKind::Principal));
        }
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && super::owner_equality::typed_id(catalog, read) { indexes.retain(|index| index.name == "byId"); }
        let [index] = indexes.as_slice() else { return };
        let keys = index.columns.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(at, column)| {
            let key = if named { read.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)) } else { read.keys.get(at) }?;
            Some(format!("{column} = {}", fact_text(&key.value)))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        // A successful ruled row lookup proves the indexed row exists. Extra
        // projected-field tests are not raw rule evidence and are deliberately omitted.
        known.insert(format!("exists {}[{keys}]", read.table));
    }
    fn point_probe(catalog: &Catalog, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
        if read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() { return false; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return false };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        info.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) }
                else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() })
use super::*;

#[test]
fn intentional_input_cases_and_later_operations_are_not_fixture_drift() {
    let p = Project::new();
    p.source("use personas alice\nquery answer(n: int 1..5):\n  return n\n");
    fs::create_dir(p.0.join("fixtures")).unwrap();
    let check_cases = [
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":6},"expect":"E_INPUT"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{},"expect":"E_INPUT"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":"wrong"},"expect":"E_INPUT"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":2,"extra":1},"expect":"E_INPUT"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":false,"expect":"E_INPUT"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":6},"expect":"denied"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":6},"expect":"refused"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":6},"expect":"failed"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":6},"expect":"NOT_ALLOWED"}"#,
    ].join("\n");
    fs::write(p.0.join("fixtures/test.cases.jsonl"), &check_cases).unwrap();
    let out = p.run(&["check", "--json"]);
    assert!(out.status.success(), "{out:?}");
    assert!(!parsed(&out)["notes"].as_array().unwrap().iter().any(|n| n["code"] == "N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT"));
    let runnable_cases = check_cases.lines().filter(|line| !line.contains("NOT_ALLOWED")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n");
    fs::write(p.0.join("fixtures/test.cases.jsonl"), &runnable_cases).unwrap();
    let test = p.run(&["test", "--fixture", "fixtures"]);
    assert!(test.status.success(), "{test:?}");
    fs::write(p.0.join("fixtures/test.cases.jsonl"), format!("{check_cases}\n{{\"op\":\"later\",\"args\":{{}}}}\n{{\"op\":\"answer\",\"args\":{{\"n\":6}},\"expect\":\"sent\"}}\n{{\"op\":\"answer\",\"args\":false,\"expect\":\"denied\"}}\n")).unwrap();
    let out = p.run(&["check", "--json"]);
    let json = parsed(&out);
    let notes: Vec<_> = json["notes"].as_array().unwrap().iter().filter(|n| n["code"] == "N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT").collect();
    assert_eq!(notes.len(), 2, "{json}");
    let test = p.run(&["test", "--fixture", "fixtures"]);
    assert!(!test.status.success());
    let text = String::from_utf8_lossy(&test.stderr);
    assert!(text.contains("\"args\" is an object"), "{test:?}");
    assert!(!p.store().exists());
}

#[test]
fn production_readiness_groups_dependents_and_collapses_persona_identity() {
    let p = Project::new();
    let source = "use identity password\ntable staff:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  insert <- exists staff[viewer]\n  online only\ntable rooms:\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- exists staff[viewer]\n  online only\ntable bookings:\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- exists staff[viewer]\n  online only\nmutation staffer(principal: principal):\n  return insert staff { principal }\nmutation room():\n  return insert rooms {}\nmutation booking():\n  return insert bookings {}\n";
    p.source(source);
    let out = p.run(&["check", "--json"]);
    assert!(out.status.success(), "{out:?}");
    let json = parsed(&out);
    let notes: Vec<_> = json["notes"].as_array().unwrap().iter().filter(|n| n["code"] == "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER").collect();
    assert_eq!(notes.len(), 1, "{json}");
    let msg = notes[0]["message"].as_str().unwrap();
    assert!(msg.starts_with("staff:") && msg.contains("bookings, rooms depend on it"), "{msg}");
    p.source(&source.replace("use identity password", "use personas alice"));
    let out = p.run(&["check", "--json"]);
    let json = parsed(&out);
    let notes: Vec<_> = json["notes"].as_array().unwrap().iter().filter(|n| n["code"] == "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER").collect();
    assert_eq!(notes.len(), 1, "{json}");
    assert!(notes[0]["message"].as_str().unwrap().starts_with("persona-only: safe to ignore without `use identity`; no production identity"));
    for seat in ["ops", "not a seat", ""] {
        p.source(&source.replace("insert <- exists staff[viewer]", &format!("insert <- viewer = 'service:{seat}' or exists staff[viewer]")));
        let out = p.run(&["check", "--json"]); assert!(out.status.success(), "{out:?}");
        let json = parsed(&out);
        let count = json["notes"].as_array().unwrap().iter().filter(|n| n["code"] == "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER").count();
        assert_eq!(count, usize::from(seat != "ops"), "{seat}: {json}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn fixture_constraints_keep_the_column_and_code_instead_of_not_found() {
    let p = Project::new();
    p.source("table proofs:\n  version: int 1..\n  label: text <=30\n  unique label else DUPLICATE\n  public 'proofs'\nquery versions():\n  return proofs first 10 by .id\n");
    fs::create_dir(p.0.join("fixtures")).unwrap();
    for (rows, expected) in [
        (r#"{"id":"p","version":0,"label":"a"}"#, "proofs.version"),
        ("{\"id\":\"p1\",\"version\":1,\"label\":\"a\"}\n{\"id\":\"p2\",\"version\":1,\"label\":\"a\"}", "DUPLICATE"),
    ] {
        fs::write(p.0.join("fixtures/proofs.jsonl"), rows).unwrap();
        let out = p.run(&["test", "--fixture", "fixtures"]);
        assert!(!out.status.success());
        let text = format!("{}{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout), String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr));
        assert!(text.contains(expected) && !text.contains("NOT_FOUND"), "{text}");
        assert!(text.contains("E_CONSTRAINT") || text.contains("DUPLICATE"), "{text}");
    }
    assert!(!p.store().exists());
}

//! The Quarry parser, ported from Snapback 2. Every refusal is a located
//! diagnostic with a rewrite; the parser recovers per line and per
//! declaration so one document yields every problem at once.

use crate::ast::*;
use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, Site, syntax, syntax_with, unsupported};
use crate::lexer::{Kind, Token, lex};
use crate::print;

mod expr;
mod predicates;
pub(crate) mod repairs;
mod views;
mod maintains;
mod timeline;
mod expected;
mod types;
mod reserved;
pub(crate) use reserved::repeated_identity;
mod programs;
mod actions;
mod routes;

pub(crate) fn precedence(op: &str) -> Option<u8> {
    Some(match op {
        "??" => 1,
        "or" => 2,
        "and" => 3,
        "=" | "!=" | "<" | "<=" | ">" | ">=" | "in" => 4,
        "+" | "-" => 5,
        "*" | "/" | "%" => 6,
        _ => return None,
    })
}

pub fn parse_document(file: &str, source: &str) -> (Document, Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    parse_with_types(file, source, Vec::new(), Default::default())
}

pub(crate) fn parse_with_types(file: &str, source: &str, known_tables: Vec<TableDecl>, known_views: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, crate::types::Ty>) -> (Document, Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    let (tokens, diagnostics) = lex(file, source);
    let source = source.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace('\r', "\n").split('\n').map(str::to_owned).collect();
    let mut parser = Parser { source, tokens, position: 0, declaration_start: 0, comparison_spans: Default::default(), expression_spans: Default::default(), diagnostics, focal_table: None, repair_args: None, known_tables, known_views, document: Document { file: file.to_owned(), ..Document::default() } };
    parser.document();
    (parser.document, parser.diagnostics)
}

struct Parser {
    source: Vec<String>,
    tokens: Vec<Token>,
    position: usize,
    declaration_start: usize,
    comparison_spans: std::collections::BTreeMap<(u32, u32), std::ops::Range<usize>>,
    expression_spans: std::collections::BTreeMap<(u32, u32), std::ops::Range<usize>>,
    diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic>,
    document: Document,
    focal_table: Option<String>,
    known_tables: Vec<TableDecl>,
    known_views: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, crate::types::Ty>,
    repair_args: Option<(Site, Vec<ArgDecl>)>,
}

const TOP_LEVEL: [&str; 19] = ["view", "use", "table", "maintain", "channel", "query", "mutation", "job", "action", "route", "effect", "shape", "fn", "proc", "cursor", "schedule", "index", "unique", "deliver"];

impl Parser {
    fn document(&mut self) {
        while !self.at_kind(Kind::Eof) {
            self.skip_newlines();
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Eof) {
                break;
            }
            let start = self.position;
            let result = self.top_level();
            if result.is_ok() && let Some(last) = self.tokens[start..self.position].iter()
                .rev().find(|t| !matches!(t.kind, Kind::Newline | Kind::Indent | Kind::Dedent | Kind::Eof)) {
                let first = &self.tokens[start].site;
                let text = self.source[first.line as usize - 1..last.site.line as usize].join("\n");
                self.document.source.declarations.insert(first.to_string(), text);
            }
            if let Err(diagnostic) = result {
                self.diagnostics.push(diagnostic);
                self.recover_top_level();
            }
        }
    }

    fn top_level(&mut self) -> Fallible<()> {
        self.declaration_start = self.position;
        self.comparison_spans.clear();
        self.expression_spans.clear();
        self.focal_table = None;
        self.repair_args = None;
        let token = self.peek().clone();
        match token.value.as_str() {
            "action" => self.parse_action(),
            "route" => self.parse_route(),
            "use" => self.parse_use(),
            "table" => {
                let table = self.parse_table()?;
                self.document.tables.push(table);
                Ok(())
            }
            "view" => self.parse_view(),
            "maintain" => {
                if self.tokens.get(self.position + 3).is_some_and(|token| token.value == "timeline") {
                    let timeline = self.parse_timeline()?;
                    self.document.timelines.push(timeline);
                    return Ok(());
                }
                let maintain = self.parse_maintain()?;
                self.document.maintains.push(maintain);
                Ok(())
            }
            "query" | "mutation" | "job" => {
                let program = self.parse_program()?;
                self.document.programs.push(program);
                Ok(())
            }
            "effect" => {
                let effect = self.parse_effect()?;
                self.document.effects.push(effect);
                Ok(())
            }
            "shape" => self.parse_shape_declaration(),
            "fn" => self.parse_function_declaration(),
            "aggregate" => {
                let line = self.source[token.site.line as usize - 1].trim_start();
                let tail = line.strip_prefix("aggregate").unwrap_or(line);
                Err(unsupported(&token.site, "aggregate declaration", format!("view{tail}")).guide("from-sql"))
            },
            "proc" => Err(unsupported(&self.peek().site, "proc", "fn")),
            "cursor" => Err(unsupported(&token.site, "cursor as a declaration", "cursor is reserved for a future Quarry form; rename this declaration")),
            "channel" => Err(unsupported(&token.site, "channel", "ephemeral channels arrive after the first slice; keep presence and typing in the client for now")),
            "schedule" => {
                let schedule = self.parse_schedule()?;
                self.document.schedules.push(schedule);
                Ok(())
            }
            "deliver" => Err(syntax_with(&token.site, "deliver is a table line spelled sync in Snapback 4", "sync to memberships[.conversationId] last 500 by .at")),
            "index" | "unique" => Err(syntax_with(&token.site, "top-level indexes are invalid; place by/unique inside the table block", "move the index line into its table block")),
            "device" | "oblivious" => Err(unsupported(&token.site, &token.value, "pass an explicit bounded argument to the operation")),
            other => Err(syntax(&token.site, format!("unexpected top-level {other:?}"))),
        }
    }

    fn parse_use(&mut self) -> Fallible<()> {
        let site = self.take("use")?.site;
        let kind = self.word()?;
        match kind.value.as_str() {
            "server" => self.document.server = true,
            "identity" => {
                let mut password = true;
                let mut guests = true;
                if self.at("password") {
                    self.position += 1;
                    guests = false;
                } else if self.at("guests") {
                    self.position += 1;
                    password = false;
                }
                if let (Some(first), Some(first_site)) =
                    (self.document.identity, self.document.identity_site.as_ref())
                {
                    return Err(repeated_identity(&site, first, first_site, (password, guests)));
                }
                self.document.identity = Some((password, guests));
                self.document.identity_site = Some(site.clone());
            }
            "personas" => {
                let first = self.word()?;
                self.document.personas.push(first.value);
                while self.maybe(",") {
                    let next = self.word()?;
                    self.document.personas.push(next.value);
                }
            }
            other => return Err(unsupported(&kind.site, &format!("use {other}"), "use server, use identity, use identity password, use identity guests, or use personas alice, bob")),
        }
        self.end_line(&site)
    }

    fn parse_table(&mut self) -> Fallible<TableDecl> {
        let site = self.take("table")?.site;
        let name = self.binding_name()?;
        self.take(":")?;
        self.newline()?;
        self.take_kind(Kind::Indent)?;
        let mut table = TableDecl { receipts: None, ephemeral: None, search: Vec::new(), name: name.clone(), columns: Vec::new(), indexes: Vec::new(), rules: Vec::new(), immutable: Vec::new(), caps: Vec::new(), final_exists: Vec::new(), retain: None, expire: None, not_before: None, public: None, sync: None, online_only: false, site };
        while !self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) && !self.at_kind(Kind::Eof) {
            self.skip_newlines();
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) {
                break;
            }
            let result = self.table_line(&mut table);
            if let Err(diagnostic) = result {
                self.diagnostics.push(diagnostic);
                self.recover_line();
            }
        }
        self.take_kind(Kind::Dedent)?;
        Ok(table)
    }

    fn table_line(&mut self, table: &mut TableDecl) -> Fallible<()> {
        let token = self.peek().clone();
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — a table keyword spelled as a column names the reserved word.
        if self.peek_at(1).value == ":" && token.kind == Kind::Word && reserved::TABLE_WORDS.contains(&token.value.as_str()) {
            return Err(reserved::column(&token.site, &token.value));
        }
        match token.value.as_str() {
            "receipts" if self.peek_at(1).value != ":" => {
                let site = self.take("receipts")?.site;
                if table.receipts.is_some() {
                    return Err(Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_RECEIPTS_CLAUSE", "duplicate receipts clause", "keep one receipts clause").guide("receipts"));
                }
                let name = self.word()?.value;
                self.take("by")?; self.take(".")?;
                let group = self.word()?.value;
                self.take(",")?; self.take(".")?;
                let position = self.word()?.value;
                self.take("from")?; self.take(".")?;
                let sender = self.word()?.value;
                self.take("to")?;
                let members = self.word()?.value;
                self.take(":")?; self.newline()?; self.take_kind(Kind::Indent)?;
                let mut clause = ReceiptsDecl {
                    member_group: group.clone(), principal: "principal".into(),
                    counter: String::new(), floor: String::new(), count: String::new(),
                    delivered_field: format!("{name}DeliveredOrdinal"), seen_field: format!("{name}SeenOrdinal"),
                    runs: format!("{name}Runs"),
                    operations: ["Receipt", "Delivered", "Seen", "Member", "Progress", "Compact"].iter().map(|suffix|format!("{name}{suffix}")).collect(),
                    name, group, position, sender, members, site: site.clone(),
                };
                let mut keys = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
                while !self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) {
                    let key = self.word()?.value;
                    if !keys.insert(key.clone()) { return Err(Diagnostic::new(&site,"E_RECEIPTS_CLAUSE","duplicate receipt mapping","keep each mapping once").guide("receipts")); }
                    match key.as_str() {
                        "member" => { self.take(".")?; clause.member_group = self.word()?.value; self.take(",")?; self.take(".")?; clause.principal = self.word()?.value; }
                        "counter" | "floor" | "count" => {
pub fn bind(documents: Vec<Document>) -> Bound {
    let mut bound = bind_unverified(documents.clone());
    crate::repair::verify_bound(&documents, &mut bound.diagnostics);
    bound
}

// Repair checks run the same planner and admission, without recursively
// verifying the diagnostics from a rejected candidate.
pub(crate) fn bind_unverified(mut documents: Vec<Document>) -> Bound {
    let mut diagnostics = Vec::new();
    let mut first_identity = None;
    for document in &documents {
        if let (Some(methods), Some(site)) = (document.identity, document.identity_site.as_ref()) {
            if let Some((first_methods, first_site)) = &first_identity {
                diagnostics.push(crate::parser::repeated_identity(
                    site,
                    *first_methods,
                    first_site,
                    methods,
                ));
            } else {
                first_identity = Some((methods, site.clone()));
            }
        }
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — catalog provenance comes from source, before any generated declarations.
    let authored_indexes = documents.iter().flat_map(|doc|
        doc.tables.iter().map(|t| (&t.name, &t.indexes))
            .chain(doc.maintains.iter().map(|m| (&m.name, &m.indexes))))
        .map(|(name, indexes)| (name.clone(), indexes.iter().map(|i| i.name.clone()).collect())).collect();
    receipts::expand(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);

//! E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR: a table rule uses an expression rules do not
//! evaluate (an ordered comparison outside insert rules and named update
//! transitions, the clock, arithmetic or a helper call). The rewrite moves the
//! author's own comparison into the mutation as `require … else CODE`.

use crate::ast::Expr;
use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Site};

/// What the rule language lacks for this term, if anything.
pub(super) fn unsupported_side(expr: &Expr) -> Option<&'static str> {
    match expr {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "now" => Some("the clock (`now`)"),
        Expr::Call { .. } => Some("a helper or function call"),
        Expr::Binary { op, .. } if matches!(op.as_str(), "+" | "-" | "*" | "/" | "%" | "??") => Some("arithmetic"),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// The effect a rule judges, as the author spelled it.
pub(super) fn effect_word(insert: bool, update: bool, read: bool) -> &'static str {
    if insert { "insert" } else if update { "update" } else if read { "read" } else { "delete" }
}

/// The refusal for one comparison `cmp` in a `table`'s `effect` rule.
pub(super) fn diagnostic(site: &Site, table: &str, effect: &str, transition: bool, cmp: &Expr, what: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(cmp));
    let field = first_field(&text).unwrap_or_else(|| "value".to_owned());
    let code = format!("INVALID_{}", screaming(&field));
    let message = format!("`{text}` in the {effect} rule of {table} uses {what}, which table rules do not evaluate: rules compare columns, viewer and literals with = and !=, and order (<, <=, >, >=) only an insert row's own required int, time, decimal or money columns and integer literals (minor units beside decimal or money), or old/next in a named update (old, next) rule");
    // The mutation names the stored row `row` and supplies new values as arguments.
    let stored = |image: Option<&str>, name: &str| match image { Some("next") => name.to_owned(), _ => format!("row.{name}") };
    let inserted = |_: Option<&str>, name: &str| name.to_owned();
    let rewrite = match effect {
        "insert" => {
            let mutation = map_fields(&text, &inserted);
            if what == "an ordered comparison" {
                format!("compare this row's own required int or time columns or an integer literal in the insert rule, or check it in the mutation that inserts the row: `require {mutation} else {code}`")
            } else {
                format!("check it in the mutation that inserts the row, using the values it inserts: `require {mutation} else {code}`")
            }
        }
        "update" if !transition && what == "an ordered comparison" => {
            let next = map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!("next.{name}"));
            format!("name both images and bound the new one: `update (old, next) <- {next}`; or check it in the mutation: `row = {table}[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` then `require {} else {code}`", map_fields(&text, &stored))
        }
        "read" if what.contains("now") => match direct_focal_field(cmp) {
            Some(expires) => format!("a read rule is an audience, which cannot change with time: hide rows past this row's stored time with `expire at .{expires}`, or filter the query (`{table} where {} …`)", map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!(".{name}"))),
            None => format!("a read rule is an audience, which cannot change with time: copy the deciding deadline into a required time column on {table} and use `expire at .expiresAt`, or filter the query (`{table} where {} …`); `expire at` accepts a local time column, not a reference hop", map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!(".{name}"))),
        },
        "read" => format!("a read rule is an audience, which cannot compare magnitudes: filter the query instead (`{table} where {} …`)", map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!(".{name}"))),
        _ => format!("check it in the mutation before the write: `row = {table}[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` then `require {} else {code}`", map_fields(&text, &stored)),
    };
    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR", message, rewrite).guide("rules")
}

/// An unnamed `update <- R` holds for the row before and after the write, so
/// its named form is `update (old, next) <- R(old) and R(next)`: every clause
/// kept, the ordered one included (lane 062). The mutation form follows.
pub(crate) fn named_update(table: &str, rule: &Expr, comparison: &str) -> String {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(rule));
    let wrap = |s: String| if text.contains(" or ") { format!("({s})") } else { s };
    let old = wrap(map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!("old.{name}")));
    let next = wrap(map_fields(&text, &|_, name| format!("next.{name}")));
    let field = first_field(comparison).unwrap_or_else(|| "value".to_owned());
    let _ = table;
    format!("name both images; an unnamed update rule holds before and after the write, so the named form keeps both: `update (old, next) <- {old} and {next}`; or drop `{comparison}` from the rule and check the new value in the mutation: `require {} else INVALID_{}`", map_fields(comparison, &|_, name| name.to_owned()), screaming(&field))
}

/// Pasteable named form for a rule that already names one image. Bare focal
/// fields in a named transition refer to the new image.
pub(crate) fn named_update_source(rule: &Expr) -> String {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — preserve authored old/next images and map bare fields to next in named transitions.
    let text = crate::print::expression(rule);
    let body = map_fields(&text, &|image, name| match image {
        Some(image) => format!("{image}.{name}"),
        None => format!("next.{name}"),
    });
    format!("update (old, next) <- {body}")
}

/// A named transition's comparison the rule language cannot type, as the
/// mutation check that states the same condition.
pub(crate) fn transition_require(table: &str, cmp: &Expr) -> String {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(cmp));
    let field = first_field(&text).unwrap_or_else(|| "value".to_owned());
    let stored = |image: Option<&str>, name: &str| match image { Some("next") => name.to_owned(), _ => format!("row.{name}") };
    format!("check it in the mutation that updates the row: `row = {table}[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` then `require {} else INVALID_{}` (the new values are the mutation's arguments)", map_fields(&text, &stored), screaming(&field))
}

/// A table rule's predicate as the check a mutation states before writing:
/// the row's fields become the values the mutation writes.
pub(crate) fn mutation_check(rule: &Expr) -> String {
    map_fields(&strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(rule)), &|_, name| name.to_owned())
}

/// `else CODE` after a table rule: the refusal of a rule is E_RULE.
pub(crate) fn rule_else(else_site: &Site, effect: &str, rule: &Expr, code: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let text = strip_parens(&crate::print::expression(rule));
    let code = if code.chars().next().is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_uppercase()) { code } else { "NOT_ALLOWED" };
    Diagnostic::new(else_site, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX",
        format!("a table rule takes no `else`: a write its {effect} rule refuses is E_RULE; only `require … else CODE` and `unique … else CODE` name a refusal"),
        format!("keep `{effect} <- {text}`, and name the refusal in the mutation before the write: `require {} else {code}`", mutation_check(rule))).guide("rules")
}

fn strip_parens(text: &str) -> String {
    let trimmed = text.trim();
    if trimmed.starts_with('(') && trimmed.ends_with(')') {
        // Only strip a pair that encloses the whole text.
        let mut depth = 0i32;
        for (i, c) in trimmed.char_indices() {
            match c { '(' => depth += 1, ')' => depth -= 1, _ => {} }
            if depth == 0 && i + 1 < trimmed.len() { return trimmed.to_owned(); }
        }
        return trimmed[1..trimmed.len() - 1].to_owned();
    }
    trimmed.to_owned()
}

fn ident(c: char) -> bool { c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_' }
fn ident_start(c: char) -> bool { c.is_ascii_alphabetic() || c == '_' }

/// The first column the comparison names: `.stars`, `old.highBid` or `next.at`.
fn first_field(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let chars: Vec<char> = text.chars().collect();
    for (i, c) in chars.iter().enumerate() {
        if *c == '.' && chars.get(i + 1).is_some_and(|c| ident_start(*c)) {
            return Some(chars[i + 1..].iter().take_while(|c| ident(**c)).collect());
        }
    }
    None
}

/// A time field owned by the focal row. Do not descend through a member
/// chain: `.hearing.releaseAt` must never become `expire at .hearing`.
fn direct_focal_field(expr: &Expr) -> Option<&str> {
    match expr {
        Expr::Member { base, name, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }) => Some(name),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => direct_focal_field(left).or_else(|| direct_focal_field(right)),
        Expr::Unary { value, .. } => direct_focal_field(value),
        _ => None,
    }
}

/// Rewrite each focal field `.f`, `old.f` and `next.f` through `map(image, f)`,
/// leaving other member accesses alone.
fn map_fields(text: &str, map: &dyn Fn(Option<&str>, &str) -> String) -> String {
    let chars: Vec<char> = text.chars().collect();
    let mut out = String::new();
    let mut i = 0;
    let name_at = |at: usize| -> String { chars[at..].iter().take_while(|c| ident(**c)).collect() };
    while i < chars.len() {
        let c = chars[i];
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — field-looking text inside a literal is data, not a row-image reference.
        // Field-looking text inside a literal is data, not syntax. Preserve
        // the original spelling, including escaped quote characters.
        if matches!(c, '\'' | '"') {
            let quote = c;
            out.push(c);
            i += 1;
            while i < chars.len() {
                let literal = chars[i];
                out.push(literal);
                i += 1;
                if literal == '\\' && i < chars.len() {
                    out.push(chars[i]);
                    i += 1;
                } else if literal == quote {
                    break;
                }
            }
            continue;
        }
        let previous = if i == 0 { None } else { Some(chars[i - 1]) };
        if c == '.' && previous.is_none_or(|p| !ident(p) && p != ')' && p != ']') && chars.get(i + 1).is_some_and(|c| ident_start(*c)) {
            let name = name_at(i + 1);
            out.push_str(&map(None, &name));
            i += 1 + name.chars().count();
            continue;
        }
        if ident(c) && previous.is_none_or(|p| !ident(p) && p != '.') {
            let word = name_at(i);
            let after = i + word.chars().count();
            if matches!(word.as_str(), "old" | "next") && chars.get(after) == Some(&'.') && chars.get(after + 1).is_some_and(|c| ident_start(*c)) {
                let name = name_at(after + 1);
                out.push_str(&map(Some(&word), &name));
                i = after + 1 + name.chars().count();
                continue;
            }
            out.push_str(&word);
            i = after;
            continue;
        }
        out.push(c);
        i += 1;
    }
    out
}

fn screaming(name: &str) -> String {
    let mut out = String::new();
    for (i, c) in name.chars().enumerate() {
        if c.is_ascii_uppercase() && i > 0 { out.push('_'); }
        out.push(c.to_ascii_uppercase());
    }
    out
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn fields_move_into_the_mutation() {
        let stored = |image: Option<&str>, name: &str| match image { Some("next") => name.to_owned(), _ => format!("row.{name}") };
        assert_eq!(map_fields(".stars >= 1 and .stars <= 5", &|_, name| name.to_owned()), "stars >= 1 and stars <= 5");
        assert_eq!(map_fields("next.highBid >= old.highBid + old.increment", &stored), "highBid >= row.highBid + row.increment");
        assert_eq!(map_fields(".deadline > now", &stored), "row.deadline > now");
        assert_eq!(map_fields("helper(.a, viewer)", &stored), "helper(row.a, viewer)");
        assert_eq!(first_field("(old.highBid + 1)"), Some("highBid".into()));
        assert_eq!(screaming("drawAt"), "DRAW_AT");
        assert_eq!(strip_parens("(.a >= 1)"), ".a >= 1");
        assert_eq!(strip_parens("(.a) + (.b)"), "(.a) + (.b)");
    }
}

   368	    pub(super) fn parse_read(&mut self, table: &str, site: &Site) -> Fallible<ReadExpr> {
   369	        self.take("[")?;
   370	        let mut keys = Vec::new();
   371	        let mut seeks = Vec::new();
   372	        while !self.at("]") {
   373	            let key_site = self.peek().site.clone();
   374	            let mut column = None;
   375	            let mut end = 1;
   376	            if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) {
   377	                while self.peek_at(end).value == "." && self.peek_at(end + 1).kind == Kind::Word { end += 2; }
   378	            }
   379	            if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && self.peek_at(end).value == "!=" {
   380	                return Err(syntax_with(&key_site,
   381	                    "probe brackets take named equalities, not != tests",
   382	                    "pin a full unique key and compare the row field outside the brackets, or use a bounded program `exists table[key] where .column != value`; program probes see only caller-readable rows, while table-rule probes see all rows; use unique/cap/after insert constraints for invariants across hidden rows"));
   383	            }
   384	            if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && self.peek_at(end).value == "=" {
   385	                column = Some(self.maintain_column()?);
   386	                self.take("=")?;
   387	            }
   388	            if column.is_none() && self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && matches!(self.peek_at(end).value.as_str(), ">" | ">=" | "<" | "<=") {
   389	                let column = self.maintain_column()?;
   390	                let op = self.peek().value.clone();
   391	                self.position += 1;
   392	                let value = self.expression(0, &[",", "]"])?;
   393	                seeks.push(SeekBound { column, op, value, site: key_site });
   394	            } else {
   395	                if !seeks.is_empty() { return Err(syntax_with(&key_site, "pin the prefix before the seek comparisons", "t[prefix, col >= start, col < end] first 50 by index")); }
   396	                let value = self.expression(0, &[",", "]"])?;
   397	                keys.push(KeyPart { column, value, site: key_site });
   398	            }
   399	            if !self.maybe(",") {
   400	                break;
   401	            }
   402	        }
   403	        self.take("]")?;
   404	        Ok(ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: table.to_owned(), keys, index: None, range: None, filter: None, matching: None, seeks, site: site.clone() })
   405	    }

use snapback4_lang::{compile, diag::SourceRewriteKind, Diagnostic};
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState, value::object};

fn refusal(source: &str, code: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    bound.diagnostics.iter().find(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == code).cloned()
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing {code}; diagnostics: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics))
}

fn emitted_source_compiles(source: &str, authored: &str, diagnostic: &Diagnostic) -> String {
    let emitted = diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_ref().expect("a verified source rewrite");
    let repaired = source.replacen(authored, emitted, 1);
    assert_ne!(repaired, source, "fixture must contain the authored source");
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired.clone())]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "emitted rewrite did not compile:\n{emitted}\n{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    repaired
}

fn classified(diagnostic: &Diagnostic, expected: SourceRewriteKind) {
    let actual = diagnostic.source_rewrite_kind.as_ref().expect("a concrete rewrite category");
    // This deliberately stays exhaustive: a new concrete rule-edit category
    // makes the golden fail to compile until it has a fixture and semantics case.
    match actual {
        SourceRewriteKind::MixedKey => assert_eq!(expected, SourceRewriteKind::MixedKey),
        SourceRewriteKind::MembershipBootstrap => assert_eq!(expected, SourceRewriteKind::MembershipBootstrap),
        SourceRewriteKind::WriteResultNarrowing => assert_eq!(expected, SourceRewriteKind::WriteResultNarrowing),
        SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate => assert_eq!(expected, SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate),
    }
}

#[test]
fn every_concrete_rule_rewrite_is_the_emitted_text_and_compiles_in_its_schema() {
    let positional = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('staff', 'member')\n  unique principal, role\n  public 'roles'\ntable records:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'staff']\n  online only\nquery visible():\n  return records first 10 by .id\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(positional, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN");
    classified(&diagnostic, SourceRewriteKind::MixedKey);
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_deref(), Some("roles[principal = viewer, role = 'staff']"));
    emitted_source_compiles(positional, "roles[viewer, role = 'staff']", &diagnostic);

    let update = "table fees:\n  status: enum('pending', 'confirmed')\n  public 'fees'\n  update <- old.status = 'pending' and next.status = 'confirmed'\n  online only\nmutation transition(fee: fees, status: enum('pending', 'confirmed')):\n  update fees[fee] { status }\n  return null\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(update, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR");
    classified(&diagnostic, SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_deref(), Some("update (old, next) <- (old.status = 'pending' and next.status = 'confirmed')"));
    emitted_source_compiles(update, "update <- old.status = 'pending' and next.status = 'confirmed'", &diagnostic);

    let partially_named = "table fees:\n  owner: principal\n  status: enum('pending', 'archived')\n  public 'fees'\n  update <- .owner = viewer and old.status != 'archived'\n  online only\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(partially_named, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR");
    classified(&diagnostic, SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
    assert_eq!(
        diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_deref(),
        Some("update (old, next) <- (next.owner = viewer and old.status != 'archived')")
    );
    let repaired = emitted_source_compiles(
        partially_named,
        "update <- .owner = viewer and old.status != 'archived'",
        &diagnostic,
    );
    assert!(repaired.contains("update (old, next)") && repaired.contains("next.owner"));

    let literal_field_text = "table fees:\n  owner: principal\n  label: text <=40\n  public 'fees'\n  update <- old.label = '.owner' and .owner = viewer\n  online only\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(literal_field_text, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR");
    classified(&diagnostic, SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
    assert_eq!(
        diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_deref(),
        Some("update (old, next) <- (old.label = '.owner' and next.owner = viewer)")
    );
    emitted_source_compiles(
        literal_field_text,
        "update <- old.label = '.owner' and .owner = viewer",
        &diagnostic,
    );

    let underscore_field = "table fees:\n  _owner: principal\n  status: enum('pending', 'archived')\n  public 'fees'\n  update <- old.status != 'archived' and ._owner = viewer\n  online only\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(underscore_field, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR");
    classified(&diagnostic, SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
    assert_eq!(
        diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_deref(),
        Some("update (old, next) <- (old.status != 'archived' and next._owner = viewer)")
    );
    emitted_source_compiles(
        underscore_field,
        "update <- old.status != 'archived' and ._owner = viewer",
        &diagnostic,
    );
}

only when the clashing row is readable. A hidden collision stays `E_RULE`,
even with `else TAKEN`. Use this example only if occupied dates and booking
references may be public. Private booking details keep their own read rule.
If the product instead permits readable availability checks, an `exists` or
`count` domain over that public availability table has a uniformly readable
audience. Pinning a private guest merely narrows the check; it cannot enforce
a constraint across all guests.

## On delete restrict

An undeclared reference is `on delete restrict`: deleting the parent refuses
E_REFERENCED while any live child names it. Declare the product behavior on
the child reference:

- `on delete restrict` keeps audit/history rows and blocks the parent delete.
- `on delete cascade` deletes children too; every parent group needs the
  declared cap or unique key that bounds the cascade. A multi-level cascade
  continues through each child's declared cascade references.
- `on delete set null` clears an optional reference.
- `on delete keep` preserves the child and its old parent id after deletion.

For a multi-level ownership tree, use cascade only where deleting the root
really deletes the descendant. Use keep or restrict at history and audit
boundaries so deleting an intermediate row does not erase records that should
survive.

## Server-arrival order within a conversation

For timestamp order with ties, use existing columns: `sync last 500 by .at, .id`
and query `last 50 by .at, .id`. No ordinal or conversation-row rewrite is needed.
For server-arrival order, client-minted ids and timestamps are insufficient.
Declare an engine sequence. A client-supplied integer can pin
the history forever by writing the largest value; `sequence by` refuses
such values with E_SEQUENCE_SUPPLIED, including on updates and upserts.

```quarry
table conversations:
  creator: principal
  public 'shared conversation'
  insert <- .creator = viewer
  online only

table members:
  conversationId: conversations
  principal: principal
  joinedAt: sequence by conversationId
  unique conversationId, principal
  public 'shared conversation membership'
  insert <- exists conversations[id = .conversationId, creator = viewer]
  online only

table messages:
  conversationId: conversations
  ordinal: sequence by conversationId
  body: text <=200
  public 'shared message history'
  insert <- exists members[.conversationId, viewer]
  online only

mutation createConversation():
  c = insert conversations { creator: viewer }
  insert members { conversationId: c.id, principal: viewer }
  return c

mutation addMember(conversationId: conversations, principal: principal):
  insert members { conversationId, principal } if absent
live contributions under the read meter; a large group can be unavailable.
The client then asks the server, which uses the backlog-bounded read. Physical
totals and private next-expiry metadata are not delivered to bypass that limit.
Live scalar misses and empty pages remain incomplete when source coverage is
incomplete. An inherited
aggregate over a public source with subset delivery runs on the server;
its local scalar and page coverage is unknown, including for the viewer.

Filtered counts disclose predicate membership, calendar groups disclose bucket
membership, and rank discloses relative values to the aggregate's read audience.
The filter grants no source-row read. N_MAINTAIN_DELTA names dependencies;
restrict the aggregate read rule when the delta is private (guide rules).
When that delta is an intended, reviewed product disclosure, record the review
without changing authority:

```quarry
maintain monthlyCounts: count escorts by zone, month(.at)
  read <- exists staff[viewer]
  disclosure reviewed 'staff intentionally see monthly zone totals'
```

The rationale silences N_MAINTAIN_DELTA for this maintain. It does not make the
source or aggregate public and does not weaken either table's write rules.

Even a hidden aggregate can disclose a constraint outcome: near an integer
endpoint, a hidden selected contribution can make a write fail with
E_OVERFLOW while an excluded contribution commits. Work reservation does not
prevent this overflow bit; the refusal names only the view and column,
and the failed transaction rolls back.

Adding or changing a filter, key, duration, offset, dependent literal default or index
is a derived rebuild at its declaration. Today's atomic activation rebuilds
populated stores before publishing the new generation; it is not resumable.
Attribution capture runs inside the same atomic activation, before that rebuild, preserving existing paths.
