4156cdf7e56fa066b8e206d9e49945dac23ab48f Add cross-runtime Wave 12 language evidence
49890d57a709ade9d8dbf7906250d420c945bfd8 Complete expiry progress and Postgres aggregate adoption
                // A row point may return null because its read meter cut. Do
                // not turn that unavailable fact into an authored NOT_FOUND.
                if self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Query && t.is_optional() && matches!(t.required(), Ty::Row(_)) {
                    e = Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::RequireUncapped, args: vec![e] };
                }
                let tmp = self.temp("present");
                out.push(Stmt::Let { name: tmp.clone(), value: e });
                out.push(Stmt::Require { condition: Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Ne, left: Box::new(ir::var(&tmp)), right: Box::new(Expr::Literal(Value::Null)) }, code: code.clone(), site: Some(site.to_string()), message: message.clone() });
                if let A::Read(read) = value.as_ref() { self.narrow_point(read); }
                Ok((ir::var(&tmp), t.required()))
            }
            A::Shape { base, fields, .. } => self.shape(base, fields, site, out),
            A::ShapeUse { base, name, .. } => {
                let Some(shape) = self.catalog.shapes.get(name).cloned() else {
                    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — diagnose SQL's trailing desc only after project-wide shape resolution.
                    if name == "desc" && let A::Read(read) = base.as_ref() && let Some(range) = &read.range {
                        let page = format!(
                            "{} {} by {}",
                            if range.order == ast::Order::Desc { "last" } else { "first" },
                            range.take,
                            if read.order_keys.is_empty() {
                                read.index.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| "<index>".into())
                            } else {
                                read.order_keys.iter().map(|key| format!(".{key}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")
                            },
                        );
                        return Err(Diagnostic::new(
                            site,
                            "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX",
                            format!("`{page}` is already {}; Quarry has no trailing `desc`", if range.order == ast::Order::Desc { "descending" } else { "ascending" }),
                            print::read_expr(read),
                        )
                        .guide("from-sql"));
                    }
                    return Err(unknown(site, format!("unknown shape {name}"), name_rewrite(name, self.catalog.shapes.keys().map(String::as_str), Some("declare `shape name = { fields }`"))));
                };
                self.shape(base, &shape.fields, site, out)
            }
            A::Walk { table, seed, column, depth, .. } => {
                let (recurse, ty) = self.walk(table, seed, column, *depth, site, out)?;
                let list = self.temp("walk");
                out.push(Stmt::Let { name: list.clone(), value: Expr::Array(Vec::new()) });
                let item = self.temp("node");
                out.push(Stmt::For { binding: item.clone(), domain: LoopDomain::Recurse(recurse), body: vec![Stmt::Push { target: list.clone(), value: ir::var(&item) }] });
    pub(super) fn index_mismatch(&self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, mode: ReadMode, keys: &[(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)], index: &ast::IndexDecl) -> Diagnostic {
        let expected = index.columns.iter().map(|col| format!("{col}: {}", self.key_ty(info, index, col).name())).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let supplied = read.keys.iter().zip(keys).map(|(part, (_, _, ty))| {
            let value = print::expression(&part.value);
            format!("{}{}: {}", part.column.as_ref().map_or(String::new(), |col| format!("{col} = ")), value, ty.name())
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let label = format!("{}.{} takes ({expected}); supplied ({supplied})", read.table, index.name);
        let arguments = keys.iter().map(|(column, _, ty)|
            (column.as_deref(), !ty.is_optional() && *ty != Ty::Unknown)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let point_admitted = |candidate: &ast::IndexDecl|
            super::super::point_key::check(info, candidate, read, &arguments, false).is_ok();
        let accepts = |candidate: &ast::IndexDecl| {
            candidate.words.is_none() && match mode {
                ReadMode::Point | ReadMode::FilteredPoint => candidate.unique && candidate.columns.len() == keys.len(),
                ReadMode::Exists => candidate.columns.len() == keys.len(),
                ReadMode::Range => candidate.columns.len() > keys.len(),
            } && keys.iter().enumerate().all(|(i, (name, expr, ty))| {
                let col = name.as_ref().unwrap_or(&candidate.columns[i]);
                candidate.columns[..keys.len()].contains(col) && (ty.comparable(&self.key_ty(info, candidate, col)) || (self.key_ty(info, candidate, col).is_identity() && matches!(expr, Expr::Literal(Value::String(_)))))
            }) && (mode == ReadMode::Range || point_admitted(candidate))
        };
        let keys_match = keys.iter().enumerate().all(|(i, (name, expr, ty))| {
            let Some(col) = name.as_ref().or_else(|| index.columns.get(i)) else { return false; };
            index.columns.iter().take(keys.len()).any(|column| column == col)
                && (ty.comparable(&self.key_ty(info, index, col)) || (self.key_ty(info, index, col).is_identity() && matches!(expr, Expr::Literal(Value::String(_)))))
        });
        if mode == ReadMode::Range && keys.len() == index.columns.len() && keys_match {
            let mut guidance = if index.unique { "use a point for one row if its key arguments are admitted".to_owned() }
                else { format!("add id as the final column of {} to range over this prefix", index.name) };
            if self.kind == ProgramKind::Job {
                guidance.push_str("; for a job chunk, scan an ordered population and filter inside the chunk body");
            }
            let changes = if index.unique && point_admitted(index) { vec![crate::repair::ReadChange::Point] } else { Vec::new() };
            return type_error(&read.site, format!("{label} pins every key, leaving no column to range over; {guidance}"), "")
                .guide("indexes").repair_read(&read.site, changes);
        }
        let same_width = match mode {
            ReadMode::Range => keys.len() < index.columns.len(),
            _ => keys.len() == index.columns.len(),
        };
        let names_match = keys.iter().all(|(name, _, _)| name.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| index.columns.iter().take(keys.len()).any(|col| col == name)));
        let reason = if !same_width { "has the wrong number of keys" } else if !names_match { "names columns outside this index prefix" } else if mode != ReadMode::Range && !index.unique && mode != ReadMode::Exists { "cannot select one row because the index is not unique" } else { "has the wrong key type" };
        // Planner tokens cannot be pasted as an authored `by` name. Point
        // alternatives must also satisfy uniqueness and the current guards.
        let alternatives: Vec<_> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|candidate| candidate.name != index.name
            && !self.catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), candidate.name.clone())) && accepts(candidate)).collect();
        let changes = if let [alternative] = alternatives.as_slice() {
            vec![crate::repair::ReadChange::Index(alternative.name.clone())]
        } else { Vec::new() };
        type_error(&read.site, format!("{label} {reason}; supply compatible arguments or select a matching index"), "")
            .guide("indexes").repair_read(&read.site, changes)
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#9 — hidden aggregate keys are nullable; choosing zero is explicit, never an automatic schema rewrite.
pub(super) fn write_rewrite(field: &ObjectField, expected: &Ty, got: &Ty) -> String {
    if let Ty::Optional(inner) = got {
        if matches!(inner.as_ref(), Ty::Int | Ty::Money(..) | Ty::Decimal(..)) && inner.writable_to(expected) {
            if let Some(value) = &field.value { return format!("{}: {} ?? 0 (only if your application treats a hidden/missing value as zero)", field.name, print::expression(value)); }
        }
    }
    fn body(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, statements: &[Stmt], mut known: BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, guard_names: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, observe_prefix: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        for statement in statements {
            match statement {
                Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => {
                    let writes = effectful(catalog, condition);
                    if writes { known.clear(); }
                    for read in absence(condition, false) {
                        guard(catalog, schema, program, &Expr::Exists { site: read.site.clone(), read }, &known, system, notes);
                    }
                    // Calls can return an existence value even when no probe is spelled here.
                    let mut copy = analysis_expr(condition);
                    super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
                        if let Expr::Shape { base, .. } = expr {
                            input_guards(catalog, schema, program, base, &known, global, system, visiting, notes);
                        }
                        for (name, shape) in definitions(catalog, expr) {
                            // In a requirement, a helper can invert an input
                            // solely in its returned expression. Inspect all
                            // actual values, not just guard-consuming definitions.
                            let inputs = match expr {
                                Expr::Call { args, .. } => args.clone(),
                                Expr::ShapeUse { base, .. } | Expr::Shape { base, .. } => vec![(**base).clone()],
                                _ => vec![],
                            };
                            for input in inputs { input_guards(catalog, schema, program, &input, &known, global, system, visiting, notes); }
                            let key = format!("{shape}:{name}");
                            if !visiting.insert(key.clone()) { continue; }
                            if let Some(inner) = helper_body(catalog, &name, shape) {
                                let entry = if let Expr::Call { args, .. } = expr && !shape { call_facts(catalog, &name, args, &known, global) } else { known.intersection(global).cloned().collect() };
                                body(catalog, schema, program, &inner, entry, global, &guard_bindings(catalog, &inner), system, true, visiting, notes);
                            }
                            visiting.remove(&key);
                        }
                    });
                    if !writes { facts(condition, &mut known); }
                }
                Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, .. } => {
                    let writes = effectful(catalog, condition);
                    if writes { known.clear(); }
                    guard(catalog, schema, program, condition, &known, system, notes);
                    expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, condition, &known, global, system, true, visiting, notes);
                    let mut yes = known.clone(); if !writes { facts(condition, &mut yes); }
                    let yes = body(catalog, schema, program, then_body, yes, global, guard_names, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
                    let no = body(catalog, schema, program, else_body, known.clone(), global, guard_names, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
                    known = yes.intersection(&no).cloned().collect();
                }
                Stmt::For { body: inner, domain, name, .. } => {
                    if effectful(catalog, domain) { known.clear(); }
                    expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, domain, &known, global, system, observe_prefix || guard_names.contains(name), visiting, notes);
                    // Any later iteration can begin after an earlier iteration's helper/write.
                    let (writes, binds) = effects(catalog, inner, &mut BTreeSet::new());
                    let mut changed = BTreeSet::new(); if binds { rebound_names(inner, &mut changed); }
                    let changed = changed.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>();
                    let mut entry = if writes { BTreeSet::new() } else { known.clone() };
                    invalidate(&changed, &mut entry);
                    invalidate(std::slice::from_ref(name), &mut entry);
                    let after = body(catalog, schema, program, inner, entry, global, guard_names, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
                    // Break/continue paths may bypass evidence near the body's end.
                    if writes { known.clear(); } else if binds { invalidate(&changed, &mut known); } else { known = known.intersection(&after).cloned().collect(); }
                }
                // Viewer scopes differ from the surrounding principal; never carry its facts in.
                Stmt::As { body: inner, principal, .. } => {
                    expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, principal, &known, global, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
                    body(catalog, schema, program, inner, BTreeSet::new(), global, guard_names, false, true, visiting, notes); known.clear();
                }
                Stmt::Break { .. } | Stmt::Continue { .. } => break,
                _ => {
                    // Values can contain conditional guards too; writes invalidate earlier role facts.
                    let one = vec![statement.clone()];
                    let wrote = effects(catalog, &one, &mut BTreeSet::new()).0;
                    // Operand order can hide the grant before a later condition in this expression.
                    if wrote && !matches!(statement, Stmt::Bind { value, .. } | Stmt::Expr { value, .. } | Stmt::Return { value, .. } if ordered_call(catalog, value)) { known.clear(); }
                    match statement {
                        Stmt::Bind { value, names, .. } => expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, value, &known, global, system, observe_prefix || names.iter().any(|name| guard_names.contains(name)), visiting, notes),
                        Stmt::Expr { value, .. } | Stmt::Return { value, .. } => expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, value, &known, global, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes),
                        Stmt::Emit { payload, once, .. } => {
                            expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, payload, &known, global, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
                            if let Some(value) = once { expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, value, &known, global, system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes); }
                        }
                        _ => {}
                    }
                    if wrote { known.clear(); }
                    else if let Stmt::Bind { names, .. } = statement {
                        invalidate(names, &mut known);
                        if let Stmt::Bind { value, .. } = statement { lookup_fact(catalog, schema, names, value, &mut known); }
                    }
                    if matches!(statement, Stmt::Return { .. }) { break; }
                }
            }
        }
        known
            let expected = column_ty(target, column).ok_or_else(|| unknown(site, format!("{} has no column {column}", read.table), name_rewrite(column, target.columns.keys().map(String::as_str).chain(["id"]), None)))?;
            if !ty.comparable(&expected) && !(expected.is_identity() && matches!(term, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(_)))) { return Err(type_error(site, format!("{column} is {}; {}.{column} is {}", ty.name(), read.table, expected.name()), format!("use {} for {column}", expected.name()))); }
            if expected.required().is_identity() && let RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(text)) = term { *term = RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone())); *ty = Ty::Id; }
            if let (Ty::Enum(variants), RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(text))) = (expected.required(), &*term) {
                if !variants.contains(text) { return Err(type_error(site, format!("'{text}' is not one of {}.{column}'s variants {}", read.table, variants.join(", ")), format!("'{}'", variants[0]))); }
            }
        }
    }
    if named.is_none() {
        let typed = types.iter().cloned().zip(terms.iter().map(|(_, term)| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(_))))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let declared = super::positional_keys::candidates(target, read, &typed, &catalog.synthesized);
        if declared.len() != 1 {
            return Err(super::positional_keys::refusal(target, read, &typed, &declared, catalog));
        }
        if read.index.is_none() {
            let mut selected = read.clone();
            selected.index = Some(declared[0].name.clone());
            return probe(catalog, info, &selected, site, bindings, extras);
        }
    }
    let mut candidates: Vec<&IndexDecl> = target
        .indexes
        .iter()
        .filter(|index| index.is_plain())
        .filter(|index| named.is_some() || !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
        .filter(|index| match &read.index {
            Some(explicit) => &index.name == explicit,
            None => true,
        })
        .filter(|index| match &named {
            Some(names) => (index.columns.len() == names.len() && names.iter().all(|n| index.columns.contains(n))) || (extras && target.total_unique(index) && index.columns.iter().all(|column| names.contains(column))),
            None => (index.unique || read.index.is_some()) && index.columns.len() == terms.len(),
        })
        .filter(|index| {
            index.columns.iter().enumerate().all(|(position, column)| {
                let ty = match &named {
                    Some(names) => names.iter().position(|n| n == column).map(|p| &types[p]),
                    None => types.get(position),
                };
                let expected = if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(read.table.clone()) } else { target.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown) };
                ty.is_some_and(|ty| ty.comparable(&expected) || (expected.is_identity() && matches!(terms[position].1, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(_)))))
            })
        })
        .collect();
    if extras && named.is_some() {
        if let Some(width) = candidates.iter().filter(|i| target.total_unique(i)).map(|i| i.columns.len()).min() {
            candidates.retain(|i| target.total_unique(i) && i.columns.len() == width);
            // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — named equalities covering two total unique keys
            // test one row either way (the other key's columns are extra
            // equalities on it), so the first declared key decides; no ambiguity.
            candidates.truncate(1);
        }
        else if candidates.iter().any(|i| i.unique) { candidates.retain(|i| i.unique); }
    }
# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, SQLite in the production CLI, one platform binary at a time. A separately enabled Postgres evaluation adapter is not a hosted/deployable target (Moves below).

        }

        let block = self.at_kind(Kind::Indent);
        if block { self.take_kind(Kind::Indent)?; }
        let mut indexes = Vec::new();
        let mut read = None;
        let mut reviewed_disclosure = None;
        let mut online_only = false;
        while block && !self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) && !self.at_kind(Kind::Eof) {
            self.skip_newlines();
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) {
                break;
            }
            let result: Fallible<()> = (|| {
                let effect = self.word()?;
                if effect.value == "where" && row_key.is_some() {
                    filter = Some(self.filtered_predicate(&source, &[], true)?);
                    return self.newline();
                }
                if effect.value == "online" {
                    self.take("only")?;
                    if online_only { return Err(syntax_with(&effect.site, "duplicate online only", "keep one online only line")); }
                    online_only = true;
                    return self.end_line(&effect.site);
                }
                if effect.value == "by" {
                    let name = self.word()?.value;
                    self.take(":")?;
                    let mut columns = vec![self.maintain_column()?];
                    while self.maybe(",") { columns.push(self.maintain_column()?); }
                    self.newline()?;
                    indexes.push(IndexDecl { name, columns, unique: false, refusal: None, words: None, computed: std::collections::BTreeMap::new(), site: effect.site });
                    return Ok(());
                }
                if effect.value == "disclosure" {
                    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — reviewed disclosure is a non-empty audit annotation, not runtime authority.
                    self.take("reviewed")?;
                    let reason = self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?.value;
                    if reason.trim().is_empty() {
                        return Err(syntax_with(
                            &effect.site,
                            "a reviewed disclosure needs a concrete rationale",
                            "disclosure reviewed 'why this aggregate delta is an intended read channel'",
                        ));
                    }
                    self.newline()?;
                    if reviewed_disclosure.replace(reason).is_some() {
                        return Err(syntax_with(
                            &effect.site,
                            "duplicate reviewed disclosure",
                            "keep one disclosure reviewed 'rationale' line",
                        ));
                    }
                    return Ok(());
                }
                if effect.value == "readable" {
                    return Err(unsupported(&effect.site, "readable on a maintain", "read <- rule  -- a maintain has one rule; the engine delivers it to its source's audience"));
                }
                if effect.value == "sync" {
                    return Err(crate::bind::audience_diagnostics::sync_syntax(&name, &source, &effect.site));
                }
                if effect.value != "read" && effect.value != "public" {
                    return Err(syntax_with(&effect.site, format!("unknown maintain property {}", effect.value), "read <- rule"));
                }
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#23 — public has the same read authority on tables and aggregate views.
                let value = if effect.value == "public" {
                    if self.at_kind(Kind::Str) { self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?; }
                    Expr::Name { name: "allow".into(), site: effect.site.clone() }
                } else { self.parse_focal_rule_value(&effect.value, &effect.site)? };
                self.newline()?;
                if read.is_some() {
                    return Err(syntax_with(&effect.site, format!("maintain {name} has more than one read rule"), "keep one read <- rule"));
                }
                read = Some(RuleDecl { effect: Effect::Read, bindings: Vec::new(), value, site: effect.site.clone() });
                Ok(())
            })();
            if let Err(diagnostic) = result {
                self.diagnostics.push(diagnostic);
                self.recover_line();
            }
        }
        if block { self.take_kind(Kind::Dedent)?; }
        Ok(MaintainDecl { row_key, filter, keys, indexes, name, aggregate: aggregate.value, source, column, by, read, reviewed_disclosure, online_only, site })
    }

#![forbid(unsafe_code)]
//! Quarry for Snapback 4: one language for tables, rules and programs
//! (LLP 2000.000 §5.1), bound to the core IR in Rust so `check` runs in
//! milliseconds with no other runtime.

pub mod ast;
pub mod bind;
pub mod diag;
pub mod emit;
pub mod guide;
pub mod lexer;
pub mod parser;
pub mod print;
pub mod source;
pub mod recipe;
mod repair;
pub mod types;
pub mod release_changes;

pub use bind::{Bound, BoundProgram, bind};
pub use diag::{Diagnostic, Site};
pub use parser::parse_document;

/// Parse and bind a set of `(file name, source)` documents.
pub fn compile(sources: &[(String, String)]) -> Bound {
    let mut documents = Vec::new();
    let mut diagnostics = Vec::new();
    for (file, source) in sources {
        let (document, mut parsed) = parse_document(file, source);
        diagnostics.append(&mut parsed);
        documents.push(document);
    }
    // Refusals still block binding. On these malformed forms only, complete
    // their repairs with column types/table names from the whole project,
    // including files and declarations that follow the offending operation.
    let repair_files: std::collections::BTreeSet<_> = diagnostics.iter().filter(|d|
        d.code == "E_QUARRY_SELF_EQUALITY" || (d.code == "E_QUARRY_UNSUPPORTED" && d.message.starts_with("count(*)")))
        .map(|d| d.site.file.clone()).collect();
    if !repair_files.is_empty() {
        let tables: Vec<_> = documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| doc.tables.iter().cloned()).collect();
        // Use the ordinary binder's projected types, including renamed and
        // computed fields. Invalid or non-scalar projections get no invented
        // argument type. This discarded binding is diagnostic-only.
        let views = if documents.iter().any(|doc| !doc.views.is_empty() || !doc.feed_views.is_empty()) {
            let mut declarations = documents.clone();
            for doc in &mut declarations {
                doc.programs.clear(); doc.actions.clear(); doc.routes.clear(); doc.schedules.clear();
            }
            bind(declarations).view_types
        } else { Default::default() };
        diagnostics.retain(|d| !repair_files.contains(&d.site.file));
        for ((file, source), document) in sources.iter().zip(&mut documents) {
            if repair_files.contains(file) {
                let (repaired, mut parsed) = parser::parse_with_types(file, source, tables.clone(), views.clone());
                *document = repaired;
                diagnostics.append(&mut parsed);
            }
        }
    }
    bind::audience_diagnostics::parser_context(&documents, &mut diagnostics);
    repair::verify(&documents, &mut diagnostics);
    if !diagnostics.is_empty() {
        return Bound { receipt_members: Default::default(), refusal_sites: Default::default(), view_types: Default::default(), server: documents.iter().any(|doc| doc.server), schema: Default::default(), programs: Vec::new(), personas: Vec::new(), diagnostics, notes: Vec::new() };
    }
    bind(documents)
}

#[derive(Default)]
pub struct Catalog {
    pub ordinary_searches: Vec<String>,
    pub source: crate::source::SourceMap,
    pub feed_views: BTreeMap<String, super::feed_view::Plan>,
    pub extremum_domains: BTreeMap<String, ReadExpr>,
    pub read_origins: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub canonical_read_origins: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub view_reads: BTreeMap<String, crate::ast::MergeExpr>,
    pub view_types: BTreeMap<String, crate::types::Ty>,
    pub planner_notes: Vec<Diagnostic>,
    /// Exact cap declarations that would admit an otherwise fully verified
    /// owner-or-membership page, keyed by the read's source site.
    pub membership_cap_repairs: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub synthesized: std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>,
    pub documents: Vec<Document>,
    pub tables: BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>,
    pub maintains: BTreeMap<String, MaintainDecl>,
    pub shapes: BTreeMap<String, ShapeDecl>,
    pub functions: BTreeMap<String, FunctionDecl>,
    pub effects: BTreeMap<String, String>,
}

impl Catalog {
    pub fn build(documents: Vec<Document>, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) -> Self {
        let mut tables: BTreeMap<String, TableInfo> = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut maintains = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut shapes: BTreeMap<String, ShapeDecl> = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut functions: BTreeMap<String, FunctionDecl> = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut effects = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut effect_sites: BTreeMap<String, Site> = BTreeMap::new();
        for doc in &documents {
            for table in &doc.tables {
                if tables.contains_key(&table.name) {
                    diagnostics.push(unknown(&table.site, format!("duplicate table {}", table.name), "rename one of the two tables"));
                    continue;
                }
                let mut columns = BTreeMap::new();
                let mut ok = true;
                for column in &table.columns {
                    if column.name == "id" {
                        diagnostics.push(unknown(&column.site, format!("table {} gets id implicitly; remove the id column", table.name), "delete this line"));
                        ok = false;
                        continue;
                    }
                    if columns.insert(column.name.clone(), column.type_.clone()).is_some() {
                        diagnostics.push(unknown(&column.site, format!("duplicate column {}.{}", table.name, column.name), "rename one of the two columns"));
                        ok = false;
                    }
                }
                for field in &table.search {
                    if !matches!(columns.get(field), Some(ScalarType { kind: TypeKind::Text { .. } | TypeKind::Enum { .. }, .. })) {
                        diagnostics.push(crate::diag::type_error(&table.site, format!("search {} requires a text or enum column on {}", field, table.name), format!("declare `{field}: text <=2000` or `{field}: enum('one', 'two')` before `search {field}`")).guide("search"));
                    }
                }
                let mut indexes = vec![IndexDecl { name: "byId".into(), columns: vec!["id".into()], unique: true, refusal: None, words: None, computed: BTreeMap::new(), site: table.site.clone() }];
                for index in &table.indexes {
                    if indexes.iter().any(|existing| existing.name == index.name) {
                        diagnostics.push(unknown(&index.site, format!("duplicate index {}.{}", table.name, index.name), "name one ordering explicitly"));
                        ok = false;
                        continue;
                    }
                    for column in &index.columns {
                        if index.computed.contains_key(column) { continue; }
                        if column != "id" && !columns.contains_key(column) {
                            diagnostics.push(crate::diag::type_error(&index.site, format!("index {}.{} names unknown column {column}", table.name, index.name), name_rewrite(column, columns.keys().map(String::as_str).chain(["id"]), None)));
                            ok = false;
                        }
                    }
                    for field in index.words.iter().flat_map(|(_, fields)| fields) {
                        match columns.get(field) {
                            Some(ScalarType { kind: TypeKind::Text { .. } | TypeKind::Enum { .. }, .. }) => {}
                            // The table stays in the catalog: one line is
                            // wrong, and its readers should hear about that
                            // line, not about a table that vanished.
                            Some(other) => diagnostics.push(crate::diag::type_error(&index.site, format!("index {}.{} tokenizes {field}, which is {}, not text or enum", table.name, index.name, crate::print::scalar_type(other)), format!("words(<a text or enum column of {}>)", table.name))),
                            None => diagnostics.push(crate::diag::type_error(&index.site, format!("index {}.{} tokenizes unknown column {field}", table.name, index.name), name_rewrite(field, columns.keys().map(String::as_str), None))),
                        }
                    }
                    for (name, expr) in &index.computed {
                        if let Err(error) = super::time_indexes::key(&table.name, &columns, name, expr) { diagnostics.push(error); }
                    }
                    indexes.push(index.clone());
                }
                let mut seen_effects = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
                for rule in &table.rules {
                    if !seen_effects.insert(rule.effect) {
                        if rule.effect == Effect::Read && table.rules.iter().any(|r| matches!(&r.value,Expr::Call{name,..} if name=="$readThrough")) {
                            diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&rule.site,"E_READ_THROUGH",format!("{} declares another read rule beside read through",table.name),"keep read through as the whole read rule; put service grants on the parent").guide("rules"));
                        } else { diagnostics.push(unknown(&rule.site, format!("duplicate {} rule on {}", rule.effect.word(), table.name), "keep one rule per effect")); }
                        ok = false;
                    }
                }
                if ok {
                    let mut decl = table.clone();
                    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — rejected search metadata must not erase the usable table symbol.
                    // An invalid search field is a fault of that search line,
                    // not evidence that every later reference names an
                    // unknown table. Keep the usable table symbol and omit
                    // only search fields that could not be indexed.
                    decl.search.retain(|field| matches!(columns.get(field), Some(ScalarType { kind: TypeKind::Text { .. } | TypeKind::Enum { .. }, .. })));
                    // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — catalog consumers see the same
                    // implicit immutability as runtime schema consumers.
                    for column in &table.columns {
                        if let Some(parent) = &column.sequence {
                            decl.immutable.push((vec![column.name.clone(), parent.clone()], column.site.clone()));
                        }
                    }
                    tables.insert(table.name.clone(), TableInfo { through: None, captures: BTreeMap::new(), recorded: BTreeMap::new(), decl, columns, indexes, maintained: None });
                }
            }
            for maintain in &doc.maintains {
                if tables.contains_key(&maintain.name) || maintains.contains_key(&maintain.name) {
                    diagnostics.push(unknown(&maintain.site, format!("maintain {} collides with a table of the same name", maintain.name), "rename the aggregate view"));
                    continue;
                }
                let mut maintain = maintain.clone();
                super::maintains::normalize_keys(&mut maintain);
                maintains.insert(maintain.name.clone(), maintain);
            }
            for shape in &doc.shapes {
                if let Some(first) = shapes.get(&shape.name) {
                    diagnostics.push(unknown(&shape.site, format!("duplicate shape {}; first declared at {}", shape.name, first.site), "rename one shape"));
                    continue;
                }
                shapes.insert(shape.name.clone(), shape.clone());
            }
            for function in &doc.functions {
                if let Some(first) = functions.get(&function.name) {
    pub(super) fn parse_range(&mut self, mut read: ReadExpr, start: usize, probe: bool) -> Fallible<ReadExpr> {
        let enclosing = self.focal_table.clone();
        let diagnostic_start = self.diagnostics.len();
        let mut predicates = Vec::new();
        let mut order = None;
        let mut take = None;
        let mut cursor: Option<Box<Expr>> = None;
        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
        let mut seen = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
        let mut violation: Option<Token> = None;
        // Non-index modifiers delimit the predicate; a bare inner `by` needs explicit scope.
        let stops = ["matching", "after", "first", "last", "by", "where", "limit", "<newline>", ":", "{", ",", "}", ")", "]"];
        let mut value_stops = stops.to_vec();
        value_stops.push("<probe-shape>");
        let mut predicate_stops = stops.to_vec();
        predicate_stops.push("<where>");
        while matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "matching" | "after" | "first" | "last" | "by" | "where") {
            let key = self.word()?;
            let rank = match key.value.as_str() {
                "matching" => -1,
                "after" => 0,
                "first" | "last" => 1,
                "by" => 2,
                _ => 3,
            };
            if violation.is_none() && rank != 3 && seen.contains(&rank) {
                violation = Some(key.clone());
            }
            seen.insert(rank);
            match key.value.as_str() {
                "where" => {
                    let where_start = self.position - 1;
                    let predicate = self.filtered_predicate(&read.table, &predicate_stops, false)?;
                    predicates.push((where_start..self.position, predicate.clone()));
                    read.filter = Some(Box::new(match read.filter.take() {
                        Some(previous) => Expr::Binary { op: "and".into(), left: previous, right: Box::new(predicate), site: key.site },
                        None => predicate,
                    }));
                }
                "matching" => {
                    // The search text: a page over the words of a `words(..)`
                    // index the text's terms are prefixes of.
                    let candidate = self.expression(0, &value_stops)?;
                    if read.matching.is_none() {
                        read.matching = Some(Box::new(candidate));
                    }
                }
                "after" => {
                    let candidate = self.expression(0, &value_stops)?;
                    if cursor.is_none() {
                        cursor = Some(Box::new(candidate));
                    }
                }
                "first" | "last" => {
                    let candidate = self.number()?;
                    if take.is_none() {
                        order = Some(if key.value == "first" { Order::Asc } else { Order::Desc });
                        take = Some(candidate);
                    }
                }
                _ => {
                    if self.maybe(".") {
                        read.order_keys.push(self.word()?.value);
                        // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — inside an object a comma may end the page: `{ notes: t where … by .at, votes: v }`.
                        while self.at(",") {
                            let next = self.peek_at(1).clone();
                            if next.value == "." {
                                self.take(",")?;
                                self.take(".")?;
                                read.order_keys.push(self.word()?.value);
                            } else if next.kind == Kind::Word && matches!(self.peek_at(2).value.as_str(), ":" | "," | "}") {
                                break;
                            } else if next.kind == Kind::Word {
                                let keys = read.order_keys.iter().map(|key| format!(".{key}")).chain(std::iter::once(format!(".{}", next.value))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                                return Err(syntax_with(&next.site, format!("each order key starts with a dot; got `{}`", next.value), format!("by {keys}")));
                            } else {
                                break;
                            }
                        }
                    } else {
                        let candidate = self.word()?.value;
                        if read.index.is_none() { read.index = Some(candidate); }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
    let d = b.diagnostics.iter().find(|d| d.code == "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT").unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{:?}", b.diagnostics));
    assert!(!d.rewrite.starts_with("First add `.sender = viewer`"), "{d}");
    assert!(d.rewrite.contains("above the entire `emit observeGap`"), "{d}");
}

#[test]
fn expiry_page_refusal_supplies_a_pasteable_helper_for_both_allocators() {
    let source = recipe();
    let helper_start = source.find("fn skipExpired_messages").unwrap();
    let helper_end = source.find("mutation skipExpiredBefore").unwrap();
    let mut source = source[..helper_start].to_owned() + &source[helper_end..];
    source.replace_range(source.find("mutation delivered(").unwrap()..source.find("query whatDidIMiss").unwrap(), "");
    for manual in [false, true] {
        for (parent, ordinal) in [("conversationId", "ordinal"), ("room", "position")] {
            let source = source.replace("conversationId", parent).replace("ordinal", ordinal);
            let source = if manual { source.replace(&format!("{ordinal}: sequence by {parent}"), &format!("{ordinal}: int\n  immutable {parent}, {ordinal}"))
                .replace("sender: viewer, expiresAt", &format!("sender: viewer, {ordinal}: 1, expiresAt")) } else { source };
            // Minimal reproduction of the recorded first-live-message gap scan;
            // the supplied artifact copy does not contain the builder's rejected source.
            let refused = source.replace(&format!("prefix = skipExpired_messages(message.{parent}, prefix, message.{ordinal})"),
                &format!("gap = first(messages where .{parent} = message.{parent} and .{ordinal} > prefix first 1 by .{ordinal})"));
            let admitted = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), refused.clone())]);
            assert!(admitted.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", admitted.diagnostics);
            assert_eq!(admitted.schema.operations["skipExpiredBefore"].predicts.as_deref(), Some("never"));
            // LLP 3000.025 admits the original initial scan. An earlier emit
            // ends that phase; keep all helper-coordinate and authority checks.
            let refused = format!("effect observeGap\n{}", refused.replace("gap = first(messages", "emit observeGap {}\n  gap = first(messages"));
            let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), refused.clone())]);
            assert_eq!(b.diagnostics.len(), 1, "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
            let d = &b.diagnostics[0];
            assert_eq!(d.code, "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT");
            assert_eq!(d.guide, "sequences");
            assert!(!d.mechanical);
            assert!(d.message.contains(&format!("{ordinal}: sequence by {parent}")), "{d}");
            assert!(d.rewrite.contains(&format!("exists messages[{parent} = scope, {ordinal} = prefix + 1]")), "{d}");
            assert!(d.rewrite.contains("range(0, 1024)"));
            let repaired = format!("{source}\n{}\n", d.rewrite);
            checked(&repaired);
        }
    }
}

#[test]
fn recipe_skips_expired_positions_but_never_live_messages_on_memory_and_sqlite() {
    for disk in [false, true] {
        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("receipt-gaps-{}", std::process::id()));
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
        let path = dir.join("store.sqlite");
        let store = if disk { Store::open(&path).unwrap() } else { Store::memory().unwrap() };
        let b = checked(&recipe());
        let mut e = Engine::new(store, b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
        write(&mut e, "createConversation", &[], &["chat", "member"], 1);
        assert_eq!(e.store().rows("members").unwrap()[0].field("joinedAt").unwrap(), &Value::Int(1));
        for (name, expiry) in [("gone", Value::Int(10)), ("live", Value::Null), ("later", Value::Null)] {
            write(&mut e, "send", &[("conversationId", id("chat")), ("expiresAt", expiry)], &[name], 2);
        }
        assert_eq!(write(&mut e, "skipExpiredBefore", &[("next", id("later"))], &[], 9), Value::Int(1));
        assert_eq!(write(&mut e, "skipExpiredBefore", &[("next", id("later"))], &[], 10), Value::Int(2));
        assert_eq!(e.store().rows("messages").unwrap().len(), 3, "expiry works before physical sweep");

        // A second member shares the same counter. Seed is a trusted fixture import.

            ("id", id("bob-member")), ("conversationId", id("chat")), ("principal", id("bob")),
            ("deliveredOrdinal", Value::Int(0)),
        ]))]).unwrap();
        write(&mut e, "send", &[("conversationId", id("chat")), ("expiresAt", Value::Int(20))], &["new"], 11);
        let count = |e: &mut Engine, now| e.query("whatDidIMiss", Context::new("bob", [("member".into(), id("bob-member"))].into()).at(now)).unwrap();
        let before = count(&mut e, 19);
        let after = count(&mut e, 20);
        assert!(before.complete && after.complete);

//! Column and parameter types, including precise unsupported-default repairs.
use super::Parser;
use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Fallible, Site, syntax, syntax_with}, lexer::Kind, print};
impl Parser {
    pub(super) fn parse_args(&mut self) -> Fallible<Vec<ArgDecl>> {
        self.take("(")?;
        let mut args = Vec::new();
        while !self.at(")") {
            let name = self.peek().clone();
            let binding = self.local_name()?;
            self.take(":")?;
            let type_site = self.peek().site.clone();
            let type_ = self.parse_type(false).map_err(|mut d| {
                if d.mechanical && d.message.starts_with("once is a column modifier") {
                    // Nested element types need a whole-list rewrite, not an argument fragment.
                    if d.site == type_site { d.site = name.site.clone(); d.rewrite = format!("{binding}: {}", d.rewrite); }
                    else { d.mechanical = false; }
                }
                d
            })?;
            if self.at("=") || self.at("default") {
                let at = self.peek().site.clone();
                self.position += 1;
                let fallback = self.expression(0, &[",", ")"])?;
                let mut optional = type_.clone(); optional.optional = true;
                return Err(syntax_with(&at, "operation parameters have no literal defaults; omission is null for an optional parameter",
                    format!("declare `{binding}: {}` and bind `{binding}Value = {binding} ?? {}` at the start of the body", print::scalar_type(&optional), print::expression(&fallback))).guide("types"));
            }
            args.push(ArgDecl { name: binding, type_, site: name.site });
            if !self.maybe(",") {
                break;
            }
        }
        self.take(")")?;
        Ok(args)
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_type(&mut self, column: bool) -> Fallible<ScalarType> {
        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
        let mut kind;
        if self.maybe("[") {
            let item = self.parse_type(column)?;
            self.take("]")?;
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — list ranges share text's inclusive spelling.
            let bounds_error = |site: &Site| syntax_with(site, "list bounds require <=N or an inclusive a..b range", "write [T] <=N or [T] a..b, for example [int] 1..8").guide("types");
            let number = |parser: &mut Self| -> Fallible<u32> {
                if parser.at("-") {
                    return Err(syntax(&parser.peek().site, "list bounds must be nonnegative integers"));
                }
                if !parser.at_kind(Kind::Number) { return Err(bounds_error(&parser.peek().site)); }
                let token = parser.take_kind(Kind::Number)?;
                if token.value.contains('.') {
                    return Err(syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers; fractional bounds are not allowed"));
                }
                token.value.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers from 0 through 4294967295"))
            };
            let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
                (0, number(self)?)
            } else {
                let min = number(self)?;
                if !self.maybe(".") || !self.maybe(".") { return Err(bounds_error(&self.peek().site)); }
                (min, number(self)?)
            };
            if min > max {
                return Err(syntax(&site, "list minimum must not exceed its maximum"));
            }
            if item.optional {
                return Err(syntax(&site, "list element types cannot be optional"));
            }
            kind = TypeKind::List { item: Box::new(item), min, max };
        } else {
            let name = self.word()?;
            kind = match name.value.as_str() {
                "text" => {
                    let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
                        (None, self.number()?)
                    } else if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) {
                        let min = self.number()?;
                        self.take(".")?;
                        self.take(".")?;
                        (Some(min), self.number()?)
                    } else {
                        // The default is normalized here; every downstream
                        // validator and resource calculation sees the same bound.
                        (None, 10_000)
                    };
                    let mut format = None;
                    if self.maybe("format") {
                        let value = self.word()?;
                        if !matches!(value.value.as_str(), "handle" | "url" | "email") {
                            let bound = match min {
                                Some(min) => format!("{min}..{max}"),
                                None => format!("<={max}"),
                            };
                            return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("unknown text format {}", value.value), format!("use text {bound} format handle, text {bound} format url, or text {bound} format email")));
                        }
                        format = Some(value.value);
                    }
                    TypeKind::Text { min, max, format }
                }

+pub(crate) fn repeated_identity(
+    site: &Site,
+    first: (bool, bool),
+    first_site: &Site,
+    repeated: (bool, bool),
+) -> Diagnostic {
+    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — identity is project-wide and repeated declarations must name both method sets.
+    let methods = |(password, guests)| match (password, guests) {
+        (true, true) => "password and guests",
+        (true, false) => "password",
+        (false, true) => "guests",
+        (false, false) => "no methods",
+    };
+    Diagnostic::new(
+        site,
+        "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX",
+        format!("`use identity` is project-wide: {} already declared at {first_site}; this repeated declaration names {}", methods(first), methods(repeated)),
+        "keep one declaration: `use identity` (password and guests), `use identity password`, or `use identity guests`",
+    )
+    .guide("production")
+}

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.
# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |

    // Immutable input-value traversal: candidate predicates determine a
    // returned row/page, not a boolean field received by a guard. Never erase
    // predicates from an AST used as readability/argument proof.
    fn value_nodes(value: &Expr, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&Expr)) {
        fn read(read: &ReadExpr, predicates: bool, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&Expr)) {
            for key in &read.keys { value_nodes(&key.value, visit); }
            for key in &read.seeks { value_nodes(&key.value, visit); }
            if predicates { for value in [&read.filter, &read.matching].into_iter().filter_map(|value| value.as_deref()) { value_nodes(value, visit); } }
            if let Some(value) = read.range.as_ref().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_deref()) { value_nodes(value, visit); }
        }
        fn fields(fields: &[ShapeField], visit: &mut impl FnMut(&Expr)) {
            for field in fields { if let ShapeField::Field { value, .. } = field { value_nodes(value, visit); } }
        }
        match value {
            Expr::Read(source) => read(source, false, visit),
            Expr::Exists { read: source, .. } | Expr::Created { read: source, .. } => read(source, true, visit),
            Expr::Unary { value, .. } | Expr::Member { base: value, .. } | Expr::Assert { value, .. }
                | Expr::ShapeUse { base: value, .. } | Expr::Walk { seed: value, .. } => value_nodes(value, visit),
            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => { value_nodes(left, visit); value_nodes(right, visit); },
            Expr::Array { items, .. } | Expr::Call { args: items, .. } => { for item in items { value_nodes(item, visit); } },
            Expr::Object { fields, .. } => { for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &field.value { value_nodes(value, visit); } } },
            Expr::Shape { base, fields: shape, .. } => { value_nodes(base, visit); fields(shape, visit); },
            Expr::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => { value_nodes(then, visit); value_nodes(condition, visit); value_nodes(otherwise, visit); },
            Expr::Write { keys, fields, condition, .. } => {
                for key in keys { value_nodes(&key.value, visit); }
                for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &field.value { value_nodes(value, visit); } }
                if let Some(value) = condition { value_nodes(value, visit); }
            },
            Expr::Merge(merge) => {
                if let Some(cursor) = &merge.cursor { value_nodes(cursor, visit); }
                for lane in &merge.lanes {
                    for value in &lane.prefix { value_nodes(value, visit); }
                    if let Some((_, value)) = &lane.loop_ { value_nodes(value, visit); }
                    if let Some(value) = &lane.distinct { value_nodes(value, visit); }
                    if let Some(shape) = &lane.project { fields(shape, visit); }
                    for source in lane.planned.iter().chain(lane.nulls.as_ref().map(|(_, source)| source)) { read(source, false, visit); }
                }
            },
            Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Focal { .. } | Expr::Literal { .. } => {}
        }
        visit(value);
    }
    fn input_guards(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, value: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        value_nodes(value, &mut |expr| expression_guard_node(catalog, schema, program, expr, known, global, system, true, visiting, notes));
    }
    fn expression_guards(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, value: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, observe_prefix: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
        if observe_prefix {
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| expression_guard_node(catalog, schema, program, expr, known, global, system, true, visiting, notes));
        } else {
            // Ordinary query values share the input-value boundary: point and
            // prefix probes in row-dependent candidate filters are visible hits.
            value_nodes(&copy, &mut |expr| expression_guard_node(catalog, schema, program, expr, known, global, system, false, visiting, notes));
        }
    }
    fn invalidate(names: &[String], known: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        known.retain(|fact| !fact.split(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() && c != '_').any(|word| names.iter().any(|name| name == word)));
    }
    fn body(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, statements: &[Stmt], mut known: BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, guard_names: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, observe_prefix: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) -> BTreeSet<String> {
    }


    fn admit(&mut self, origin: &Origin, site: &str) {

                        _ => format!("move this pass to a job: `for row in {}:`", scan.table),
                    }
                } else { "pass a bounded list argument directly, or move the population pass to a job sweep".into() };
                self.error = Some(fanout(scan, site, &reason, &rewrite));
            }
        }
    }
