//! Bind key operands once, retaining authored sequencing and contextual literals.
use super::*;

impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
    pub(super) fn bind_key_parts(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, end: usize,
        keys: &mut Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
        for (position, part) in read.keys.iter().enumerate().take(end).skip(keys.len()) {
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let expected = part.column.as_deref().or_else(|| read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)).and_then(|i| i.columns.get(position)).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column));
            let (e, t) = match expected {
                Some(expected) => self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup, &expected)?,
                None => self.expr(&part.value, &mut setup)?,
            };
            self.sequence_setup(keys.iter_mut().map(|(_, e, _)| e), setup, out);
            let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
            keys.push((part.column.clone(), e, t));
        }
        self.contextualize_bound_keys(info, read, keys)?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Apply the selected index's identity context to operands already bound
    /// for type-directed positional selection. This changes only their
    /// retained IR/type and never evaluates the authored expression again.
    pub(super) fn contextualize_bound_keys(&self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr,
        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {
        if let Some(index) = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)) {
            for (position,(column,value,ty)) in keys.iter_mut().enumerate() {
                if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().or_else(|| index.columns.get(position).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|c| key_identity(info,c)) {
                    if let Expr::Literal(Value::String(text)) = value {
                        if text.is_empty() {
                            return Err(type_error(read.keys[position].value.site(), "an empty text literal cannot be an id", "use a non-empty id literal, or null"));
                        }
                        *value = Expr::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone()));
                        *ty = expected.required();
                    } else {
                        (*value,*ty) = self.row_identity(value.clone(),ty.clone(),&expected);
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    // Mixed-key normalization names an already-bound positional prefix. Only
    // a literal acquires new context; compound expressions keep their IR/setup.
    pub(super) fn name_bound_keys(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr,
        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {
        for ((column, expr, ty), part) in keys.iter_mut().zip(&read.keys) {
            *column = part.column.clone();
            if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column))
                && matches!(&part.value, A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(_), .. }) {
                (*expr, *ty) = self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup::Body::default(), &expected)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn key_identity(info: &TableInfo, column: &str) -> Option<Ty> {
    let ty = if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()) }
        else { Ty::from_scalar(info.columns.get(column)?) };
    ty.required().is_identity().then_some(ty)

/// A bound operation: the IR plus the types the client sees.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct BoundProgram {
    /// Source statement ownership stays beside flat IR, like diagnostic sites.
    pub(crate) statement_ends: Vec<usize>,
    pub(crate) maintenance_scopes: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub view_types: BTreeMap<String, Ty>,
    pub(crate) filters: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    /// Whole-domain consumers for diagnostics only; absent from IR/wire.
    pub(crate) constraint_domains: std::collections::BTreeSet<String>,
    pub(crate) sources: BTreeMap<String, (Site, String)>,
    pub(crate) receipt_sources: Vec<(Expr, Site)>,
    pub(crate) receipt_returns: Vec<(String, Site)>,
    pub(crate) implicit_write_results: Vec<Expr>,
    /// Source-table pages before binding filters, for executable horizon repairs only.
    pub(crate) page_sources: BTreeMap<String, ReadExpr>,
    pub(crate) canonical_sources: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub(crate) selections: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    /// Scan sites the compiler generated for a timeline read: the only scans whose
    /// filter is exactly the platform's retirement check.
    pub(crate) timeline_scans: std::collections::BTreeSet<String>,
    pub program: Program,
    pub(crate) presence: Option<snapback4_core::schema::Execution>,
    pub module: String,
    pub args: Vec<(String, Ty)>,
    pub result: Ty,
    pub notes: Vec<Diagnostic>,
    pub site: Site,
    /// Job helpers with reads that need a non-uniform meter in a query too; never IR/wire.
    pub job_bound_helpers: std::collections::BTreeSet<String>,
}

impl BoundProgram {
    pub fn filter_source(&self, site: &str) -> Option<&str> { self.filters.get(site).map(String::as_str) }
    pub fn domain_source(&self, site: &str) -> Option<&str> { self.sources.get(&format!("domain/{site}")).map(|(_, text)| text.as_str()) }
    pub fn loop_source(&self, binding: &str) -> Option<&str> { self.sources.get(&format!("for/{binding}")).map(|(_, text)| text.as_str()) }
    pub fn selections(&self) -> &BTreeMap<String, String> { &self.selections }
    /// Whether the scan at `site` is a timeline read's own expansion, not an authored scan.
    pub fn timeline_scan(&self, site: &str) -> bool { self.timeline_scans.contains(site) }
    /// The actual binder choice, shared by `why` and structural cost reports.
    pub fn selection(&self, site: &str) -> Option<&str> { self.selections.get(site).map(String::as_str) }
}

pub(crate) struct Owned { pub kind: Owner, pub by: String }
pub(crate) type Ownership = BTreeMap<String, Owned>;

// @ref LLP 3000.007#19.1 — ownership travels beside flat baseline statements, never through their lowering or serialization.
#[derive(Default)]
pub(super) struct Body {
    pub code: Vec<Stmt>,
    pub ownership: Ownership,
}

impl std::ops::Deref for Body {
    type Target = [Stmt];
    fn deref(&self) -> &[Stmt] { &self.code }
}

impl Body {
    pub fn push(&mut self, stmt: Stmt) { self.code.push(stmt); }

    pub fn nest(&mut self, prefix: &str, ownership: &Ownership) {
        self.ownership.extend(ownership.iter().map(|(path, owner)| (format!("{prefix}.{path}"), Owned { kind: owner.kind, by: format!("{prefix}.{}", owner.by) })));
    }

    pub fn extend(&mut self, other: Body) {
        let offset = self.len();
        self.ownership.extend(other.ownership.into_iter().map(|(path, owner)| {
            (shift(&path, offset), Owned { kind: owner.kind, by: shift(&owner.by, offset) })
        }));
        self.code.extend(other.code);
    }

    pub fn push_if(&mut self, condition: Expr, then_body: Body, else_body: Body) {
        self.nest(&format!("{}.then", self.len()), &then_body.ownership);
        self.nest(&format!("{}.else", self.len()), &else_body.ownership);
        self.push(Stmt::If { condition, then_body: then_body.code, else_body: else_body.code });
    }

    pub fn push_for(&mut self, binding: String, domain: LoopDomain, body: Body) {
        self.nest(&format!("{}.loop", self.len()), &body.ownership);
        self.push(Stmt::For { binding, domain, body: body.code });
    }

    pub fn own(&mut self, start: usize, owner: Owner) {
        // More deeply nested owners remain: an emit's setup resets effect
        // control even when that emit occurs in another condition's helper.
        let by = self.len().to_string();
        for index in start..self.len() {
            let entry = self.ownership.entry(index.to_string()).or_insert_with(|| Owned { kind: owner, by: by.clone() });
            if owner == Owner::Payload { *entry = Owned { kind: owner, by: by.clone() }; }
        }
    }
}

/// Translate only the side map through baseline helper-return lowering.
/// No branch here can change the code that helper_returns receives or emits.
/// Original statement ownership includes a new guard around that statement;
/// nested ownership follows it into :then. A return's two assignments share
/// its ownership, and a loop's generated break has no expression owner.
pub(super) fn helper_ownership(body: &Body) -> Ownership {
    if body.ownership.is_empty() { return Ownership::new(); }
    let mut paths = BTreeMap::new();
    helper_paths(&body.code, "", "", &mut paths);
    let mut result = Ownership::new();
    for (path, owner) in &body.ownership {
        for target in &paths[path].cover {
            result.insert(target.clone(), Owned { kind: owner.kind, by: paths[&owner.by].statement.clone() });
        }
    }
    result
}

fn shift(path: &str, offset: usize) -> String {
    let (head, tail) = path.split_once('.').map_or((path, String::new()), |(head, tail)| (head, format!(".{tail}")));
    format!("{}{tail}", head.parse::<usize>().unwrap() + offset)
}

struct Location { cover: Vec<String>, statement: String }

fn child(prefix: &str, index: usize) -> String {
    if prefix.is_empty() { index.to_string() } else { format!("{prefix}.{index}") }
}

fn helper_paths(body: &[Stmt], source: &str, target: &str, paths: &mut BTreeMap<String, Location>) -> bool {
    let mut may_return = false;
    let mut offset = 0;
    for (index, stmt) in body.iter().enumerate() {
        let from = child(source, index);
        let to = child(target, offset);
        let inner = if may_return { format!("{to}.then.0") } else { to.clone() };
        let returns = match stmt {
            _ => self.assignable_to(expected),
        }
    }

    /// Whether two values may meet in `=`, `!=` or a key position.
    pub fn comparable(&self, other: &Ty) -> bool {
        let a = self.required();
        let b = other.required();
        a == b || a.assignable_to(&b) || b.assignable_to(&a) || a == Ty::Null || b == Ty::Null
    }

    /// The type both branches can share. Row-kind conflicts are errors at
    /// their first join, including inside objects and collections.
    pub fn join(&self, other: &Ty) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {
        self.join_inner(other, false)
    }

    /// Nonempty list items must never erase a conflict into Unknown: a later
    /// item could otherwise recover a type the earlier values do not have.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.1 — literals and concatenation share sound item joins.
    pub(crate) fn join_item(&self, other: &Ty) -> Result<Ty, JoinError> {
        self.join_inner(other, true)
    }

    fn page_problem(&self, scan: &Scan) -> Option<String> {
        let Some(table) = self.schema.table(&scan.table) else { return Some(format!("missing table {}", scan.table)); };
        let Some(index) = table.index(&scan.index) else { return Some(format!("missing index {}.{}", scan.table, scan.index)); };
        if !decided(self.schema, self.program, scan) { return Some("the read rule is not uniform over the prefix".into()); }
        if self.nonuniform.is_some() { return Some("another non-uniform scan can truncate this mutation's population".into()); }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.9 — a never-predicting pass drops completeness, never uniformity.
        if self.schema.operations.get(&self.program.name).is_some_and(|fact| fact.predicts.as_deref() == Some("never")) {
            // Expiry was checked once on original scan identities above;
            // these flow copies only check the remaining page shape.
            return plain_prefix_problem(self.schema, scan);
        }
        let pinned: Vec<_> = index.columns().into_iter().take(scan.prefix.len()).collect();
        let cap = self.schema.group_bound(&scan.table, &pinned);
        let Some(cap) = cap else { return Some(format!("{} has no cap over the selected group", scan.table)); };
        if !decided(self.schema, self.program, scan) { return Some("the read rule is not uniform over the prefix".into()); }
        if !matches!(scan.take, Bound::Literal(n) if n >= cap) { return Some(format!("the literal page bound must cover the schema cap {cap}")); }
        if scan.after.is_some() || scan.before.is_some() || scan.range.is_some() { return Some("a cursor or key range cuts the population".into()); }
        if self.nonuniform.is_some() { return Some("another non-uniform scan can truncate this mutation's population".into()); }
        None
    }

fn join_env(env: &mut BTreeMap<String, Origin>, other: BTreeMap<String, Origin>) {
    let keys: BTreeSet<_> = env.keys().chain(other.keys()).cloned().collect();
    for key in keys {
        let a = env.remove(&key).unwrap_or(Origin::Other);
        let b = other.get(&key).cloned().unwrap_or(Origin::Other);
        env.insert(key, a.join(b));
    }
}

fn shape_target(body: &[Stmt]) -> Option<&str> {
    let mut target = None;
    for statement in body {
        match statement {
            Stmt::Push { target: name, .. } if target.is_none() => target = Some(name.as_str()),
            Stmt::Const { .. } | Stmt::Let { .. } => {}
            _ => return None,
        }
    }
    target
}

    writes: usize,
    pushes: usize,
    top: bool,
    empty_declaration: bool,
}

impl Binding {
    fn single_top(&self) -> bool {

// @ref LLP 3000.025#6-diagnostics-and-exact-documentation-changes — name the enclosing statement, not an inferred product invariant.
fn expiry_problem(program: &Program, scan: &Scan, boundary: std::ops::Range<usize>, never: bool, in_prefix: bool) -> Problem {
    let mut message = format!("{} scans expiring {}", program.name, scan.table);
    let mut rewrite = if never { String::new() } else { format!("Make `{}` `: server`. ", program.name) };
    if in_prefix {
        message.push_str(" but may predict");
    } else if let Some(statement) = program.body[boundary.clone()].last() {
        let group = &program.body[boundary.clone()];
        let (inside, _) = crate::ir::body_read_structure(group);
        let enclosing = inside.iter().any(|candidate| std::ptr::eq(*candidate, scan));
        let site = match statement {
            Stmt::If { .. } => "if".into(),
            Stmt::For { .. } => "for".into(),
            Stmt::Emit { effect, .. } => format!("emit {effect}"),
            Stmt::Const { name, .. } | Stmt::Let { name, .. } | Stmt::Assign { name, .. } if !name.starts_with('$') =>
            {
                format!("binding {name}")
            }
            Stmt::Update { table, .. } => format!("update {table}"),
            Stmt::Upsert { table, .. } => format!("upsert {table}"),
            Stmt::WriteTarget { target, upsert, .. } => format!("{} {}", if *upsert { "upsert" } else { "update" }, target.table),
            Stmt::Delete { table, .. } => format!("delete {table}"),
            Stmt::DeleteTarget { target } => format!("delete {}", target.table),
            Stmt::Insert { table, .. } | Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { table, .. } => {
                format!("insert {table}")
            }
            _ => format!("statement {}", boundary.start + 1),
        };
        let effect = if super::read_only::statements::<true>(group) { "an emit" } else { "a write" };
        message.push_str(&if enclosing {
            format!(" inside `{site}` containing {effect} (an untaken arm counts)")
        } else {
            format!(" after `{site}` containing {effect}, ending the read-only phase")
        });
        if enclosing && let Stmt::For { .. } = statement {
            let single = Program { body: vec![statement.clone()], ..program.clone() };
            let writes = crate::ir::write_tables(&single);
            let action = writes.first().map_or("…", |(_, kind)| *kind);
            rewrite.push_str(&format!("Bind before this entire loop: `page = {} …`; `for row in page: {action} …`.", scan.table));
        } else {
            rewrite.push_str(&format!("Move this scan into its own statement above the entire `{site}`. Then write."));
        }
    }
    Problem { code: "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT", site: scan.site.clone(), page_site: Some(scan.site.clone()), message, rewrite: rewrite.trim().into() }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.025#3-proposed-rule — expiry alone may pass the server read-only prefix; matching and merges keep their refusal.
fn plain_prefix_problem(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<String> {
    let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
    if !table.index(&scan.index)?.is_plain() || scan.prefix.iter().any(|key| matches!(key, IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(_))) {
        Some("matching word scans expand hidden words before deciding visibility; read it in a query, or use a plain prefix scan".into())
    } else if scan.merge_site.is_some() {
        Some("a merge is not a plain prefix scan; read it in a query, or use a job chunk".into())
    } else { None }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#14 — ruling 17 (c): binding a page has the same abort channel as looping over it.
fn require_uniform_mutation_pages(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, scans: &[&Scan]) -> Result<(), Problem> {
    for scan in scans {
        if schema.table(&scan.table).and_then(|table| table.index(&scan.index)).is_some() && !decided(schema, program, scan) {
            return Err(fanout(Some(scan), &scan.site, "the mutation page's read rule is not uniformly decided by its prefix", &uniform_rewrite(schema, scan)));
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

/// The page's verdict is one for every row: its prefix decides the read rule,
/// or a dominating `require` of a readable grant does (`rules::grant_dominated`).
// @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — a staff-only pass after `require exists staff[viewer]` reads every row it walks.
fn decided(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, scan: &Scan) -> bool {
    scan_visibility(schema, scan).uniform || crate::engine::rules::grant_dominated(schema, program, scan)
}

fn fanout(scan: Option<&Scan>, site: &str, reason: &str, rewrite: &str) -> Problem {
    let reason = reason.split_once("; ").map_or(reason, |(reason, _)| reason);
    let population = scan.map(|scan| format!("{} by {} (page bound {}): ", scan.table, scan.index, scan.take.maximum())).unwrap_or_default();
    Problem { code: "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT", site: site.into(), page_site: scan.map(|scan| scan.site.clone()), message: format!("{population}{reason}"), rewrite: format!("{rewrite}; for a single row use a point read by id (`table[id]`); for a global count/total read a maintained aggregate whose read rule explicitly admits the caller (public only when its totals may be public)") }
}
